## Administrative/regulatory information and recommendations on the procedure

## 1.1. Scientific advice

Table 1: Scientific advice and protocol assistance

| Date              | Topic (quality/ non- clinical/ clinical)   | Reference number / Coordinator(s)   | Brief summary of the advice                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
|-------------------|--------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| 19 September 2019 | Non-clinical and Clinical                  | EMEA/H/SA/4242/2/2 019/III          | The Scientific advice pertained to the following non-clinical, and clinical aspects: ● Requirements for pharmacology and toxicology studies ● Additional non-clinical long-term toxicology studies in rabbits and minipigs ● Acceptability of the clinical data from the studies conducted in the US. ● Secondary endpoints and non-inferiority (NI) comparisons for BCVA with a NI margin |
| 16 September 2021 | Clinical                                   | EMEA/H/SA/0000061 549               | of 3.9 letters The Scientific advice pertained to the following clinical aspects: ● proposed voluntary Post-Authorization Safety Study (PASS) to evaluate the PDS safety profile in the real-world setting, to integrate the PRAC feedback in the responses to the D120 Questions.                                                                                                         |

## 1.2. Eligibility to the centralised procedure

The applicant Roche Registration GmbH submitted on 22 July 2025 an application for marketing authorisation to the European Medicines Agency (EMA) for Susvimo (Ranibizumab), through the centralised procedure falling within the Article 3(1) and point 1 of Annex of Regulation (EC) No

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

726/2004.

The applicant applied for the following indication:

Susvimo is indicated in adult patients for the treatment of neovascular (wet) age related macular degeneration (AMD) who have achieved stable disease after previously responding to intravitreal treatment with a vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) inhibitor medication.

## 1.3. Legal basis and dossier content

## The legal basis for this application refers to:

Article 8.3 of Directive 2001/83/EC - complete and independent application

The application submitted is composed of administrative information, complete quality data, and nonclinical and clinical data based on applicant's own tests and studies.

Pursuant to Article 7 of Regulation (EC) No 1901/2006, the application included an EMA Decision(s) CW/0001/2015 on the granting of a of a class waiver for neovascular age-related macular degeneration (nAMD).

## 1.5. Information on orphan market exclusivity

## 1.5.1. Similarity with authorised orphan medicinal products

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No 141/2000 and Article 3 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 847/2000, the applicant did not submit a critical report addressing the possible similarity with authorised orphan medicinal products from the start of the procedure because there is no authorised orphan medicinal product for a condition related to the proposed indication.

## 1.5.2. New active substance status

The applicant did not request the active substance ranibizumab contained in the above medicinal product to be considered as a new active substance.

## 1.5.2.1. CHMP recommendation on new active substance status

It is considered that ranibizumab is not a new active substance, as it is a constituent of a medicinal product previously authorised within the European Union. Ranibizumab is contained in the marketing authorisation of Lucentis which was authorised in the Union on 22 January 2007.

## 1.7. CHMP outcome

## 1.7.1. Opinion

Based on the CHMP review of data on quality, safety and efficacy, the CHMP considers by consensus that the benefit-risk balance of Susvimo is favourable in the following indication(s):

Susvimo is indicated in adult patients for the treatment of neovascular (wet) age-related macular degeneration (nAMD) who have achieved stable disease after previously responding to intravitreal treatment with a vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) inhibitor medicinal product

The CHMP, therefore, recommends the granting of the marketing authorisation subject to the conditions described in the following sections.

## 1.7.2. Conditions or restrictions regarding supply and use

Medicinal product subject to restricted medical prescription (See Annex I: Summary of Product Characteristics, section 4.2).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 1.7.3. Other conditions and requirements of the marketing authorisation

## 1.7.3.1. Periodic safety update reports

The requirements for submission of periodic safety update reports for this medicinal product are set out in the list of Union reference dates (EURD list) provided for under Article 107c(7) of Directive 2001/83/EC and any subsequent updates published on the European medicines web-portal.

The marketing authorisation holder shall submit the first periodic safety update report for this product within 6 months following authorisation.

## 1.7.4. Conditions or restrictions with regard to the safe and effective use of the medicinal product

## 1.7.4.1. Risk management plan (RMP)

The marketing authorisation holder (MAH) shall perform the required pharmacovigilance activities and interventions detailed in the agreed RMP presented in Module 1.8.2 of the marketing authorisation and any agreed subsequent updates of the RMP.

An updated RMP should be submitted:

- At the request of the European Medicines Agency;
- Whenever the risk management system is modified, especially as the result of new information being received that may lead to a significant change to the benefit/risk profile or as the result of an important (pharmacovigilance or risk minimisation) milestone being reached.

## 1.7.4.2. Additional risk minimisation measures

Prior to the launch of Susvimo in each Member State the MAH must agree about the content and format of the final educational programme, including communication media, distribution modalities, and any other aspects of the programme, with the National Competent Authority.

The MAH shall provide information on implant insertion procedure, implant removal procedure and refill-exchange procedure to ophthalmologists experienced in vitreoretinal surgery, with the aim to establish consistency in following the instructions for use and confidence in the surgical procedures associated with Susvimo. The refill-exchange procedure information will also be provided to ophthalmologists who will perform the refill-exchange procedure for Susvimo. The MAH shall also provide patient educational materials.

The MAH shall ensure that, following discussions and agreements with the National Competent Authority in each Member State where Susvimo is marketed, at launch and after launch, all ophthalmological clinics where Susvimo is expected to be used are provided with an up-to-date physician guide.

The physician guide will contain the following elements:

- Physician information
- Instruction video and pictograms on vitreoretinal surgery and performing the port delivery system (PDS) procedures
- Patient guide

The physician information will include the following key elements:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Sterile techniques to minimise risk of infection
- Techniques for the vitreoretinal surgery and performing the PDS procedures
- Patient monitoring after the vitreoretinal surgery and the PDS procedures
- Key signs and symptoms of vitreoretinal surgery related adverse events
- Management of adverse events related to vitreoretinal surgery and performing the PDS procedures

The patient educational materials are provided in written and audio format, and will include the following key elements:

- How to prepare for Susvimo treatment
- What are the steps following treatment with Susvimo
- Key signs and symptoms of serious adverse events including endophthalmitis
- When to seek urgent attention from the health care provider

## 2. Introduction

## 2.1. Therapeutic context

Age-related macular degeneration (AMD) is a chronic, progressive disease of the macula and a leading cause of central vision loss among people over the age of 50 years. Neovascular age-related macular degeneration (nAMD) is a late form of AMD characterized by choroidal neovascularization (CNV), a pathological outgrowth of new vessels from the choroid that leads to quick central vision loss and potential blindness. Prevalence of late, sight threatening AMD is anticipated to increase from 2.8 to 3.7 million people in Europe between 2020 and 2040.

The current standard of care in nAMD corresponds to intravitreal (IVT) injections of anti-vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) agents. These treatments provide significant recovery of vision and subsequent maintenance of that vision, while reducing the likelihood of blindness. Patients can be treated with anti-VEGF injections as often as monthly for nAMD control. Less-than-monthly injection regimens are possible for some patients; however, they still require frequent eye examinations and office visits to achieve the patient's best visual outcomes. Real-world data suggest that this burden of frequent IVT injections and office visits contributes to many patients not achieving or maintaining vision outcomes comparable to those seen in clinical trials (Cohen et al. 2013, Finger et al. 2013, Holz et al. 2015). The Port Delivery System with ranibizumab (PDS) has been developed to address this unmet medical need by reducing the overall visit and treatment burden, while achieving and maintaining optimal visual outcomes with a well characterized safety profile.

The PDS is an intraocular drug delivery system that consists of ranibizumab 100 mg/mL (Susvimo) and an ocular implant and ancillary devices (Contivue) for the surgical implantation (insertion tool assembly), initial fill (initial fill needle), refill-exchange procedure (refill needle), and explant procedure (explant tool), if required.

After being filled with the customized formulation of ranibizumab, the PDS implant is surgically implanted into the eye and subsequently refilled at 6-month intervals (i.e., every 24 weeks [Q24W]) with the customized formulation of 100 mg/mL ranibizumab to allow for sustained release and a maintained ranibizumab vitreous concentrations within the range experienced with monthly intravitreal injections (Section 1.4.1). Continuous delivery of ranibizumab via the PDS implant, with a prolonged fixed period between refill-exchange procedures represents a new, alternative treatment option that may result in a less frequent treatment regimen and patient monitoring schedule compared to IVT treatment regimen. The decrease in treatment burden may increase compliance and will help reduce the treatment burden observed with IVT injections of anti-VEGF agents for patients, caregivers, and the health care system while achieving and maintaining optimal visual outcomes.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.2. Aspects of development

A full initial MAA for Susvimo for the treatment of patients with nAMD was previously submitted on 13 April 2021 (Reference EMEA/H/C/005610) with a start of procedure on 20 May 2021. In parallel, PDS device applications were submitted to a European Union (EU) Notified Body (NB) Technischer Überwachungsverein Süd (TÜV SÜD) by a third-party manufacturer, Aesculap AG, in May 2021.

During the initial MA procedure, the D120 Assessment Report was issued on 20 September 2021 and the applicant responded to the related List of Questions on 10 December 2021. The D180 Assessment Report was issued on 1 March 2022.

Following cases of septum dislodgement reported in the PDS clinical trial program, Roche issued a Safety Memo to investigators on 28 February 2022 and shared this with the EMA. On 26 April 2022 an EMA Clarification meeting occurred to discuss four main topics: 1) quality related questions, 2) PASS design, 3) the septum dislodgement incidents and the impact on the CE Mark timelines and subsequent EU MAA timelines and 4) the future Periodic Benefit-Risk Evaluation Report schedule.

An updated D180 Assessment Report was provided by EMA on 5 May 2022 reflecting the Roche Safety Memo related to emerging cases of septum dislodgement in the PDS clinical studies. This updated D180 Assessment Report included a new multidisciplinary Major Objection related to those septum dislodgement cases.

Ultimately, the device applications were withdrawn by Aesculap AG in March 2023, and the MAA was subsequently proactively withdrawn in May 2023 (EMA/206018/2022), after the Day 180 (D180) assessment milestone, due to the inability to provide the required (European Community [EC]) Certificates/Declaration of Conformity for the PDS devices as part of the responses to the Day 180 LoOI.

A thorough investigation of the septum dislodgement cases, including a root cause analysis, was conducted and remediation actions involving component level and manufacturing changes to the PDS implant and refill needle were implemented. Evaluation of the potential impact of these changes on device performance, with appropriate in vitro testing, was successfully completed. The updates to the implant and refill needle have successfully mitigated the risk of septum dislodgement and are not expected to affect the functionality or safety of the PDS. This assessment is based on no changes to the implant materials, shape and size of the finished device, Release Control Element, or surgical procedures. Overall, the design changes have no direct impact on the drug release from the implant. Therefore, the efficacy (and safety) results from the clinical trials remain valid.

Roche subsequently decided to become the legal manufacturer of the PDS. Roche submitted applications for the conformity assessment of the devices under the brand name Contivue to TÜV SÜD in March 2024. At a pre-submission interaction in June 2024, the EMA deemed it acceptable to proceed with the drug filing (Susvimo) following the completion of the Clinical Evaluation Certification Process and the audit for the Contivue PDS devices. While the NB TÜV SÜD successfully completed its review of the technical documentation, including clinical documents, and an on-site audit for all PDS devices, the Expert Panel, in January 2025, recommended that additional safety and clinical performance data for the updated implant be provided before Conformité Européenne(CE) certification. In June 2025, Roche submitted additional information to TÜV SÜD to complement the Clinical Evaluation Report. The EC certificates for the devices are now available.

Roche is hereby submitting to EMA a new initial MAA for ranibizumab 100 mg/mL (Susvimo) to be used in conjunction with the Contivue devices.

The purpose of this MAA is to seek approval of Susvimo for the treatment of adult patients with neovascular age-related macular degeneration (nAMD). The efficacy, safety, and benefit-risk profile are supported by data from the:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Pivotal Phase III Study GR40548 (Archway; study completed; last patient last visit [LPLV] 23 June 2021).
- Dose-ranging Phase II Study GX28228 (Ladder; study completed; LPLV 28 March 2019).
- Long-term Phase III open-label extension Study GR40549 (Portal; study ongoing; clinical cutoff date [CCOD] 31 January 2025).

## Scientific advice/Protocol assistance

EMA scientific advice for the PDS in nAMD was requested by Roche in June 2019, and written feedback was received from CHMP on 24 Sep 2019 (EMEA/H/SA/4242/2/2019/III) on the non-clinical and clinical development of ranibizumab.

From a non-clinical point of view, it was agreed that the pharmacology and toxicology of ranibizumab are considered well known and no particular studies with ranibizumab alone are needed. Additional non-clinical long-term toxicology studies showing the tolerability of the PDS implant in rabbits and minipigs were supported.

From a clinical point of view, the primary purpose was to seek guidance on the acceptability of the clinical data from the Phase II and Phase III studies conducted in the US for registration of the PDS in Europe. In response to the feedback provided by the EMA, Roche added the following secondary endpoints and non-inferiority (NI) comparisons for BCVA with a NI margin of 3.9 letters: change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Weeks 36 and 40, averaged over Weeks 44 and 48, and averaged over Weeks 88 and 92. These endpoints were considered key secondary endpoints for the EMA filing purpose with a type I error control plan.

With regard to the pivotal trial GR40548 Archway, it was agreed by the CHMP that subjects enrolled should have received and been found responsive to prior treatment with anti-VEGFs. The requirement of 3 or more previous IVT injections plus a mandatory boost injection at the screening visit was supported. Furthermore, it was anticipated that this should be reflected in the label. In addition, a noninferiority margin below 4 letters was advised. It was stressed that the overall PDS safety profile was expected to reflect the combined effects of ranibizumab, the surgical implantation procedure, the implant as such, the refill procedure, and a potential explanation procedure. Furthermore, the applicant was advised to detail not only severity, but also durability of these SAEs and their impact on BCVA.

Overall, it was recognized that a potential advantage of the PDS is a less frequent dosing regimen. However, a superior safety profile of the PDS would have to be demonstrated before this argument could be accepted.

The applicant also sought written advice from the EMA on 21 September 2021

(EMA/SA/0000061549) in parallel to the ongoing MAA review regarding a proposed voluntary PostAuthorization Safety Study (PASS) to evaluate the PDS safety profile in the real-world setting, to integrate the PRAC feedback in the responses to the D120 Questions.

## 2.3. Description of the product

Susvimo (i.e., the totality of the PDS consisting of ranibizumab 100 mg/mL and the devices) is an innovative drug delivery system that includes a surgically implanted, refillable, intraocular device uniquely designed for continuous delivery of a customized formulation of ranibizumab (100 mg/mL), as well as ancillary devices for the surgical implantation, initial fill, in situ refill-exchange procedure, and explant procedure (if required). After being filled with a customized formulation of ranibizumab, the implant is surgically inserted into the eye and subsequently refilled at 6-month (Q24W) intervals to provide continuous delivery of ranibizumab directly into the vitreous over time.

The primary mode of action of the PDS is the pharmacologic activity of the ranibizumab drug product.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The PDS has the same active substance as the commercial IVT injection (ranibizumab); however, it differs in one formulation excipient and protein concentration.

The PDS devices will not be marketed separately from the PDS ranibizumab drug product, as the drug product and devices are specifically designed to be used together to achieve the desired therapeutic result.

Table 4: Components of the Port Delivery System (from Clinical Overview)

| Drug Constituent        | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|-------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Ranibizumab, 100 mg/mL  | Ranibizumab is the Fab of a recombinant humanized monoclonal anti- VEGF antibody. It consists of a 214-residue light chain linked by a disulfide bond at its C-terminus to the 231 residue N-terminal segment of the heavy chain. Ranibizumab is not glycosylated and has a molecular mass of 48,000 Da. |
| Device Constituent      | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Implant                 | Provides continuous release of ranibizumab to the vitreous over time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Insertion Tool Assembly | Facilitates handling of the implant during initial filling and implantation procedures (consists of an IT handle and IT carrier).                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Initial Fill Needle     | Fills the implant with ranibizumab prior to insertion.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Refill Needle           | Refills (in situ) the implant with ranibizumab when needed (every 24 weeks for nAMD patients).                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Explant Tool            | Grasps and securely holds the implant flange during explantation (if needed).                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

Fab = antigen binding fragment; IT = insertion tool; VEGF = vascular endothelial growth factor.

Susvimo received Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approval on 22 October 2021 and is currently commercialized in the US.

The applicant proposes the following indication:

Susvimo is indicated in adult patients for the treatment of neovascular (wet) age-related macular degeneration (AMD) who have achieved stable disease after previously responding to IVT treatment with a vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) inhibitor medication.

Susvimo is intended for continuous administration to the vitreous via the Contivue implant only. The initial fill and implant procedure and implant removal procedure must be performed by an ophthalmologist experienced in vitreoretinal surgery and trained in the implant procedures. The implant refill-exchange procedure should be performed by an ophthalmologist trained in the implant refill-exchange procedure.

Treatment with Susvimo is initiated when disease stability after IVT anti-VEGF medication is achieved as determined by the treating physician based on anatomic and/or visual parameters.

The recommended dose of Susvimo is 2 mg (0.02 mL of solution) delivered via the Contivue implant with refill-exchange procedures administered every 24 weeks (approximately 6 months).

## 2.4. Inspection issues

## 2.4.1. Good manufacturing practice (GMP) inspection(s)

Valid GMP certificates are available for all sites responsible for manufacture, in-process, release and stability testing of ranibizumab PDS drug substance and drug product. The certificates are presented as Annex 5.9 to eAF in Module 1.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

No inspection was required.

## 2.4.2. Good laboratory practice (GLP) inspection(s)

No inspection was required.

## 2.4.3. Good clinical practice (GCP) inspection(s)

The applicant states that all studies were conducted in accordance with the principles of Good Clinical Practice (GCP) (the ICH guidelines on good clinical practice [ICH E6], the US FDA regulations, the Declaration of Helsinki [October 1996], and applicable local, state, and federal laws, as well as other applicable national legal requirements). The studies were approved by the appropriate Ethics Committees and Institutional Review Boards, were audited for GCP and were source document verified.

In this marketing authorization application, clinical studies contributing to the efficacy and safety evaluation of the PDS in nAMD include one pivotal Phase III Study (GR40548, Archway) and two supportive studies (an open-label long-term extension Study GR40549, Portal, and a Phase II Study GX28228, Ladder). Archway and Ladder studies have been conducted in the United States. The first four cohorts of Portal have been conducted in the US enrolling patients from Ladder (cohorts 1 and 3) and Archway (cohorts 2 and 4). Clinical sites have been opened for GR40549 (Portal) outside the US from cohort 5 onward to enrol patients from study WR42221 (Velodrome). However, data from patients who rolled from WR42221 (Velodrome) over to the GR40549 (Portal) study are not part of the MAA.

The applicant confirms that all clinical trials carried outside the European Union meet the ethical requirements of the EU Clinical Trial Directive [2001/20/EC].

Based on the review of the clinical data, no need for a GCP inspection of the clinical trials included in this dossier was identify.

## 3. Quality aspects

## 3.1. Introduction

Ranibizumab, the active substance in Susvimo, is a humanised monoclonal antibody (MAb) fragment produced in E. coli expression vector by standard recombinant DNA technology. It is a known active substance contained in other EU centrally authorised medicinal products.

Ranibizumab is formulated with histidine hydrochloride, polysorbate 20, sucrose and water for injections.

The finished product is presented as a solution for injection in a single-dose glass vial (strength 100 mg/mL) for intravitreal use. Susvimo is intended for continuous release into the vitreous using the Contivue intraocular implant only.

Each vial contains 39.5 mg of ranibizumab in 0.395 mL solution. This provides a usable fill volume of 0.1 mL, which is required to deliver a single dose of 0.02 mL solution containing 2 mg of ranibizumab into the Contivue implant.

The Contivue implant is part of a Port Delivery System (PDS) provided separately which also contains various ancillary devices used to:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Fill the implant (initial fill needle (IFN), blue cap);
- Insert the implant (insertion tool assembly (ITA));
- Refill (refill needle (RFN), clear cap);
- Explant the implant (explant tool (ET), procedure not described in the product information since removal of the implant, if clinically necessary, is not part of the administration of the medicinal product).

During the initial fill and implant procedure, a 1 mL Luer Lock Tip syringe (not included in Contivue) is used to withdraw the medicinal product from the vial through a transfer needle (19-gauge, 1½ inch, not included in Contivue). The initial fill needle is then attached to the syringe and used to fill the Contivue implant with 0.02 mL of Susvimo solution for injection immediately. Once the implant is filled, it is inserted into the eye through the pars plana.

The refill-exchange procedures take place every 24 weeks. The Contivue implant is refilled with 0.02 mL of replacement Susvimo solution for injection via administration through the implant septum, which is covered by the conjunctiva and Tenon's capsule. A Luer Lock Tip syringe is used to withdraw the medicinal product from the vial through a transfer needle. The refill needle is then attached to the syringe and inserted into the implant to exchange its contents with replacement Susvimo.

Ranibizumab PDS in Susvimo cannot be substituted with other formulations of ranibizumab.

This application contains a full Module 3 dossier and prior knowledge based on ranibizumab intravitreal contained in Lucentis (EMEA/H/C/000715) is used.

## 3.2. Active substance

## 3.2.1. General information

Ranibizumab (INN), referred to as ranibizumab PDS, is the antigen-binding fragment (Fab) of a humanised MAb based on a human IgG1 framework. The recombinant antibody fragment is produced in E. coli and consists of one heavy chain (HC) (231 amino acid residues) and one light chain (LC) (214 amino acid residues). The amino acid sequences of both heavy and light chain are provided and complementarity-determining regions (CDRs) are highlighted. Ranibizumab does not have the typical N-linked glycosylation site (Asn297) since it does not contain the Fc region. The calculated molecular mass of intact ranibizumab is 48,379 Da (peptide chains only). The expected intrachain and interchain disulphide linkages were confirmed (five cysteine residues per light chain, five cysteine residues per heavy chain).

The biological activity of ranibizumab PDS is binding to all known biologically active forms of VEGF and, thereby, preventing binding to their receptors VEGFR-1 and VEGFR-2. As an antibody fragment, ranibizumab does not have antibody-mediated effector functions.

The provided general information for the antibody fragment ranibizumab is considered sufficient.

## 3.2.2. Manufacture, process controls and process development

## Description of manufacturing process and process controls

Ranibizumab PDS active substance is manufactured at Roche Singapore Technical Operations, Pte. Ltd (RSTO) located in Singapore. All sites involved in manufacture and quality control of the active substance operate in accordance with EU GMP.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Manufacture of ranibizumab PDS active substance consists of the thaw of a single working cell bank (WCB) ampoule, followed by fermentation, harvest, and purification. One batch of active substance is defined as the material generated from the harvest of a single production culture, followed by protein recovery and purification, resulting in formulated active substance.

Ranibizumab is expressed in E. coli cells and produced in a fed batch process. A single primary inoculum is used to produce a single secondary inoculum, which then produces a single production culture. The cell broth is cooled and further processed.

After the production culture, the fermentation broth containing host E. coli cells (whole cell broth) is disrupted by homogenisation, which releases the product from the periplasmic space into the solution. It is then clarified by centrifugation and filtration.

Critical controls for the main fermentation are bacteriophage assay and non-host contamination, which have to be negative. Other process parameters are defined as target and acceptable ranges.

The antibody is purified from the concentrate using chromatography, viral inactivation, filtration, dilution and conditioning. The conditioned UFDF pool is filtered through a sterilising-grade membrane filter into bags to produce the ranibizumab PDS active substance.

The manufacturing process including process parameters and in-process controls (IPCs) is described in sufficient detail. Reprocessing is not allowed except for refiltration once per batch under defined circumstances, which is considered acceptable.

Columns sizes and run conditions, including maximum protein load and elution criteria, are specified as target and acceptable ranges. Critical process parameters are identified and process specifications are set accordingly.

## Control of materials

A tabulated overview of compendial and non-compendial raw materials used in the production process is provided. In-house specifications for non-compendial raw materials are in place. There are no raw materials of human origin used during the manufacture of ranibizumab PDS. Raw materials of animal or recombinant origin are N-Z-Amine, Type A and tryptone. Sufficient information on quality and control of the raw- and process materials was provided.

Ranibizumab was derived from a humanised and affinity-maturated version of muMAb VEGF A.4.6.1, a recombinant murine antibody directed against VEGF. E. coli was transformed with the expression plasmid and a purified culture of the transformed cells was used to prepare a master cell bank (MCB). An acceptable protocol for qualification of new WCBs and stability monitoring of MCB and WCB is available. Suitable end-of-production cells (EPC) were established and tested to justify the allowed in vitro cell age.

## Control of critical steps and intermediates

Process parameter target and acceptable ranges are defined and critical process parameters (CPPs) are identified. In-process testing is performed and limits are identified as action limits or acceptance criteria, respectively. Control measures are in place for bacteriophages and non-host organisms in the fermentation broth, bioburden, endotoxin, protein concentration and polysorbate 20 content throughout the manufacturing process. Analytical methods for in-process testing have been validated. The presented IPC testing strategy is acceptable and consistent results were shown during process performance qualification (PPQ).

## Process validation and/or evaluation

The presented process validation data support the conclusion that the ranibizumab PDS active substance manufacturing process reliably produces the active substance meeting its predefined specifications of quality attributes.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The active substance manufacturing process was validated at the RSTO facility. The data from the PPQ campaign showed that all quality attributes met their acceptance criteria, all key performance indicators and IPCs met their process performance acceptance criteria. Process parameters (CPPs and non-CPPs) were maintained within their acceptable ranges - except for the UFDF step. Automation set points were corrected for the subsequent runs in all cases. The use of incorrect settings in the affected runs had no impact to product quality. Overall, process performance was consistent among the PPQ batches.

Process consistency was assessed for the steps that are identical between ranibizumab PDS and ranibizumab intravitreal. The assessment considered KPI data from ranibizumab PDS batches from the PPQ campaign, ranibizumab PDS clinical batches and ranibizumab intravitreal commercial batches.

Ranibizumab PDS specific steps were validated.

In addition, prior knowledge was used for assessment of the UFDF step which was leveraged from other molecules with similar processes.

After the validation campaign, the process was further improved. a targeted validation approach was used, and previously executed process validation studies remain applicable.

Process hold-times were validated by physicochemical stability studies and microbial validation studies.

Process-related impurities (including ECP) and product variants have been demonstrated to be removed to an acceptable level in the AEX pool for ranibizumab intravitreal. ..

Ranibizumab PDS active substance is shipped to the finished product production site via air and ground. For the air shipping, qualification studies demonstrate that the shipping process is capable of maintaining frozen active substance inside the storage container at the recommended storage temperature For the ground shipping, the operational qualification demonstrated the ability of the shipping system to maintain the internal temperature after equilibration.

Overall, process validation for ranibizumab PDS active substance is satisfactory.

## Manufacturing process development

For the intended commercial process, the final protein concentration in active substance was lowered. No other changes were made. Comparability exercises were performed to bridge the different processes.

For the comparability exercise comparing process material, the product quality data from the active substance batches from the PPQ campaign were compared. . Parameters assessed include size-related variants, charge-related variants, potency and pH. Results demonstrate that all quality attributes are highly similar between processes. In addition, a side-by-side comparisons of comparator batches, PPQ batches, and the reference standard demonstrated that the PPQ batches have product quality similar to batches. The degradation profile of process material was further evaluated in a comparative stress study

In another comparability exercise, material was compared. Batch analysis testing (SE-HPLC, IE-HPLC, non-reduced CE-SDS, and potency) and extended characterisation (electrospray ionisation mass spectrometry (ESI-MS) and Lys-C peptide mapping by liquid chromatography mass spectrometry (LCMS)) of the physicochemical properties of the materials was performed as a part of this assessment. The materials can be regarded similar.

. Small-scale characterization studies were conducted to evaluate the effects of a changed filter used in the purification process on product quality attributes. These studies assessed various operating parameters to ensure no adverse impact on the product. The small-scale filter model was verified against manufacturing scale to ensure representativeness. This involved comparing key performance indicators (KPIs) and product quality attributes between small-scale and manufacturing-scale runs. Multiple lots of the filter were assessed for consistency in performance.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The filter showed no adverse impact on relevant product QAs, including size-related variants, chargerelated variants, and oxidation-related variants, under the tested operating conditions. The small-scale filter characterization studies were found to be representative of manufacturing scale, based on comparisons of key performance indicators and product quality attributes. Consistent differential pressure profile trends were observed across different filter lots, with no adverse impact on product quality, indicating low lot-to-lot variability.

A comprehensive comparability exercise was performed to assess the impact of the filter on active substance quality attributes. Overall, the introduction of the filter into the ranibizumab PDS active substance manufacturing process did not adversely affect product quality attributes.

## Characterisation

## Critical quality attribute (CQA) assessment

A risk ranking filtering (RRF) approach was used to identify and classify product quality attributes (QAs) as CQAs or non-CQAs - whereby a CQA is a quality attribute having a (potential) impact on product safety or efficacy, and a non-CQA is a quality attribute that is not expected to have an impact on safety or efficacy.

The RRF tool assesses the impact and uncertainty score of each product quality attributes in the following categories: bioactivity, pharmacokinetics, immunogenicity and safety. Risk ranking is performed by considering the impact and the uncertainty of that impact. Each is ranked on a low, medium, high scale.

## Elucidation of Structure and other Characteristics

The primary structure, higher order structures, and the biological activity of ranibizumab PDS were sufficiently evaluated using a series of biochemical, biophysical, and functional characterization techniques.

Primary structureMolecular masses of the LC, HC and intact Fab were determined Secondary/higher order structureAll five expected disulfide bonds were confirmed Higher order structure was further analysed by Fourier transform infrared (FTIR) spectroscopy Heterogeneity and product related variants:

Size variants were determined by SE-HPLC Other properties:

Minor protein impurities were determined by Peptide Mapping Biological activityFunctional analysis of boinduced vascular permeability assay in guinea pigs. Overall, a comprehensive characterisation of ranibizumab PDS, in line with EMA/CHMP/BWP/532517/2008, has been presented. The results of the physicochemical and structural characterisation confirmed that ranibizumab PDS is a highly pure, homogeneous protein with the same general structure, post-translational modifications (PTMs) and other characteristics of a typical human IgG1 antibody fragment derived from E. coli cells. The results of the biological and immunochemical characterisation confirmed that ranibizumab PDS binds with high affinity to the VEGF receptor binding site. Product-related variants were sufficiently characterized and the impact of the process on product-related variants was evaluated during process validation. The relevant product-related variants (i.e., size and charge variants) are controlled at release. The removal of process-related impurities was demonstrated during process validation. The characterisation is therefore considered adequate.

## Impurities

Due to the difficulty in isolating some individual molecular variants to perform full characterisation and differentiate between the desired product, potential product-related substances (variants with the same properties as the desired product), and potential product-related impurities, all variants are grouped under the category 'product-related variants.' Product-related variants include size and charge variants. They have been sufficiently characterised and are controlled at release. Processrelated impurities include ECP, residual DNA, raw materials, raw material process-related impurities, residual solvents, and elemental impurities. Contaminants include bioburden and endotoxins.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

ECP, bioburden and endotoxins are controlled at release. Removal of other process-related impurities has been demonstrated during process validation.

## 3.2.3. Specifications

## Specifications

Specification for ranibizumab PDS active substance include control of identity, purity and impurities, potency and other general tests.

Ranibizumab PDS active substance specifications are adequate. The release specifications include tests for appearance, identity, purity and impurities, quantity, potency, microbial safety and general attributes. Ranibizumab PDS active substance (and finished product) is formulated with polysorbate 20 as a stabiliser. The polysorbate 20 concentration is tested at active substance release.

The stability specification for ranibizumab PDS active substance comprises a reduced set of parameters: physical state, pH, osmolality, identity, ECP, protein content, biobureden, bacterial endotoxins and polysorbate 20 are not monitored. Since no change in these parameters is expected for these parameters, this is acceptable. In addition, polysorbate 20 is controlled at release and stability on finished product level. Overall, the set of release parameters tested complies with ICH Q6B, Ph. Eur. 2031, and EMA/CHMP/BWP/532517/2008.

## Analytical procedures and validation

For the compendial methods (colour, clarity/opalescence, pH, bacterial endotoxins, bioburden) reference was made to the respective Ph. Eur monographs. For both the compendial and the internal methods, a reference to the respective in-house standard operating procedure (SOP) and a brief description of the method has been provided. For methods used for both active substance and finished product, reference has been made to section P.5.2.

Both commercial ranibizumab for intravitreal injection and ranibizumab PDS have the same active ingredient. Original method validation and transfer reports were provided.

Non-compendial analytical procedures were validated according to ICH Q2 (R1). All results have passed the acceptance criteria specified in the method validation protocol. Compendial methods were verified. For bioburden testing was performed by the membrane filtration method and challenge organisms, including in-house isolates have been used. All challenge organisms could be recovered.

## Batch analyses

Batch release data are presented for nineteen PPQ campaign active substance batches manufactured according to the intended commercial manufacturing process. In addition, batch release data are presented for PPQ campaign active substance batches manufactured according to the intended commercial manufacturing process with filter.

Overall, the batch release data shows consistent and comparable quality of ranibizumab PDS active substance manufactured across all batches of process. All the active substance batches comply with the pre-established specifications valid at the time of testing. The quality of the manufactured active substances is comparable between processes.

## Reference standards or materials

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The section on reference standards has been appropriately addressed. A history of reference standards used during development of ranibizumab for port delivery system is provided. A two-tiered reference standard system, consisting of a primary and a secondary reference standard, was established for ranibizumab PDS active substance and finished product. The primary reference standard will be used to qualify subsequent secondary reference standards. The secondary reference standard is used for testing in all assays requiring a reference standard.

Both the primary reference standard and secondary reference standard were produced from ranibizumab intravitreal active substance batches, which were manufactured from the ranibizumab intravitreal commercial process and are representative of ranibizumab intravitreal pivotal batches.

The primary and secondary reference standards were qualified by release and extended characterisation testing. All specifications were met.

The potency of the commercial primary reference standard was determined by multiple independent measurements relative to the initial clinical reference standard.The potency of the secondary reference standard was determined by multiple independent measurements relative to the primary reference standard

The primary reference standard is currently assessed bi-annually for stability. For potency, stability is assessed by testing a qualified product control against the secondary reference standard. The product control has an experimentally determined initial mean potency value that is used to evaluate system suitability for routine testing.

## Container closure system

Ranibizumab PDS active substance is stored in single-use bags. During storage, the product also contacts parts of the fill-in and drain ports. For stabilisation and protection, the active substance storage container is placed in a rigid handling system. The active substance storage container is delivered pre-assembled, cleaned, and gamma sterilized.

The specifications of the bag along with the technical drawings are provided. To assess leachables and extractables for the active substance storage container, extractables data provided by the vendor were reviewed. Based on the results of this study, a leachables study was conducted. The single-use bags were characterized for volatile, semi-volatile, non-volatile, acetate, formate and elemental leachables.

Based on the presented data, the container closure system appears suitable for the storage of ranibizumab PDS active substance.

## 3.2.4. Stability

The proposed long-term storage condition for ranibizumab PDS active substance is -20°C. A shelf-life is proposed based on long-term primary stability data (real-time recommended storage conditions) generated from clinical batches and PPQ batches. All batches were manufactured at RSTO and stored in PE single-use bags representative of the manufacturing-scale storage container.

Long-term and accelerated stability data for PPQ active substance batches (100 mg/mL) are available Furthermore, stability data at accelerated conditions are available

Supportive stability data is provided for technical batches. These batches were manufactured in the pilot plant with the same process and at the same scale. The protein content in these batches is the same as that in the active substance formulation (100 mg/mL) or the active substance formulation Long-term stability data for clinical batches are available. Stability data at accelerated conditions are available

Stability protocols, analytical procedures and stability specifications are provided. Compared to release specifications, the stability acceptance criterion for 'Sum of LMW Forms' and the stability acceptance criteria for charged species are wider. Stability acceptance criteria for clinical batches differ from acceptance criteria for shelf-life setting.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In addition, stress stability studies were performed on PPQ batch and technical batch to further elucidate the likely degradation products and establish the degradation pathways. The technical batch can be considered worst-case.

A comparative stress stability study was performed on PPQ batch and clinical batch. Similar degradation profiles were observed supporting the comparability of the clinical process and the commercial process.

The available stability data for samples stored at the recommended storage conditions (-20°C) show good stability

The presented data for ranibizumab PDS active substance manufactured according to the clinical and commercial processes support the intended shelf-life at the intended storage condition of-20°C.

In addition, stability studies were conducted to assess the stability of the active substance manufactured with the filter. Long-term stability studies at -20°C showed no change in the active substance. The accelerated stability studies showed changes consistent with historical data. The sideby-side comparative stability studies showed comparable degradation profiles and stability behaviour between the PPQ batches and the control batches). The PPQ batches show little to no decrease of polysorbate 20 content, while the control batches manufactured without the filter show a decrease of polysorbate 20.

The Applicant is recommended provide the long-term stability data for the intended shelf-life for the filter PPQ batch, once available. (REC). A post-approval stability commitment has been provided.

The proposed shelf-life at the intended storage condition (-20°C) is acceptable.

## 3.3. Finished medicinal product

## 3.3.1. Description of the product and pharmaceutical development

## Description of the product

The finished product is provided as a sterile liquid solution for injection in a single-use 2 mL vial (USP/Ph. Eur./JP Type I glass). It is a clear to slightly brown aqueous solution. The Applicant provided adequate information for the composition of ranibizumab PDS finished product The excipients for the formulation are compliant with the requirements of the Ph. Eur. and are commonly used in parenteral medicinal products. Ranibizumab PDS finished product is formulated as 100 mg/mL ranibizumab in sucrose, histidine HCl, polysorbate 20, pH Sucrose is used as tonicity agent and cryoprotectant. Histidine is used as buffer to maintain solution pH. Polysorbate 20 is used to prevent aggregate formation.

The container closure system consists of a Type I borosilicate 2 mL glass vial with a fluororesinlaminated rubber stopper and crimped with an aluminium seal fitted with a plastic flip-off cap. Susvimo is intended for intravitreal use with the PDS (Contivue). The PDS devices consist of an intraocular implant, insertion tool, initial fill needle, refill needle and explant tool - which are available separately. Relevant CE certificates and Declarations of conformity were provided.

## Pharmaceutical development

## Formulation development

Selection of formulation was based on prior development knowledge and commercial experience from ranibizumab intravitreal finished product containing histidine HCl, α,α-trehalose dihydrate, and polysorbate 20 This liquid formulation was developed to support ranibizumab concentrations to meet phase II clinical dosing requirements. Subsequently, a second formulation was developed to support ranibizumab concentrations of to meet phase III clinical dosing requirements. The same formulation is used for both clinical (phase III) and commercial finished products.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A formulation screening study was conducted at stress conditions. A different protein concentration was chosen for the commercial formulation. A target pH was selected for the formulation. Histidine at was selected as buffer in the finished product based on its ability to reduce the formation of degradation products.

The vial configurations were selected based on facility fit. The commercial finished product is provided in a 2 mL Type I clear glass vial with a fluororesin, laminated butyl rubber stopper and an aluminum seal with plastic flip-off cap. The target fill volume for ranibizumab PDS finished product vials is 0.395 mL. The label claim (nominal volume) is 0.1 mL (10 mg), which is the amount required to ensure the complete 2 mg initial dose for implant refill-exchange. The fill volume was confirmed by an extractable study.

To evaluate the robustness of the finished product formulation, a multi-parameter formulation design of experiment (DOE) study was conducted. The following three formulation parameters were studied to evaluate their impact on product quality attributes over the shelf life of ranibizumab PDS: pH, polysorbate 20 content, and histidine HCl content.

An in-line synthetic depth filter) was implemented at the UFDF purification step to mitigate enzymemediated degradation of polysorbate 20 during shelf life.

## Manufacturing process development

During development of ranibizumab PDS finished product different manufacturing process versions comprising different configurations formulations and ranibizumab concentrations (were used. All processes were based on the intravitreal ranibizumab commercial process. Ranibizumab PDS finished product was manufactured at Genentech SSF for the processes. For commercial process, manufacture was transferred to (VLA). Only minor adaptions have been made. Due to the lower concentration for finished product, the dilution step after thawing and homogenization of active substance could be omitted.

Comparability was assessed for the 100 mg/mL commercial finished product produced at the VLAand the 100 mg/mL phase III pivotal clinical finished product produced at the initial site The exercise demonstrates that the change of active substance process version, manufacturing facility, primary container closure components, and fill volume do not adversely affect product quality, and the finished products are considered comparable in terms of quality, safety, and efficacy. The comparability exercise included the PPQ batches clinical 100 mg/mL batches and clinical batch, which was placed on stability. Furthermore, comparability of finished product from the process which was used in clinical phase II trials with phase III material and thus in turn comparability with the commercial material was demonstrated.

## Microbiological attributes

The microbiological quality and sterility of ranibizumab PDS finished product are adequately controlled during routine manufacturing.

## Container Closure System

The primary packaging components (vial and stopper) for ranibizumab PDS finished product are standard, pharmaceutical-grade components. The 2 mL glass vial and fluororesin-laminated rubber stopper meet pharmacopeial requirements for container closure. Compatibility of the finished product solution with the components of the primary packaging is verified by stability studies at the recommended storage condition of 2-8°C. Extractables studies were conducted for the rubber stopper and the glass vial. Leachables that were identified in the extractable studies were defined as target leachables and assessed in the leachables testing. To date, leachables data from finished product vials stored for the intended shelf-life are not yet available. None of the leachables associated with the finished product container closure system is considered to be of toxicological concern from a local or systemic perspective at the doses associated with ranibizumab PDS. Ongoing leachables studies to further characterize potential patient exposures to leachables will be continued until end of shelf life.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Compatibility

Ranibizumab finished product solution is a ready-to-use solution administered by either an initial fill needle (IFN) or refill needle (RFN) into the PDS implant. The solution from the vial is initially withdrawn using a commercially available disposable syringe and transfer needle. The transfer needle is replaced with either the IFN or RFN immediately prior to administration of the solution into the PDS implant.

Compatibility of ranibizumab PDS finished product with the initial fill needle and the refill needle has been shown. The physicochemical stability of ranibizumab PDS finished product was evaluated with disposable syringes made of polypropylene (PP) and polycarbonate (PC), equipped with stainless steel transfer needles to mimic handling in the clinical setting. After withdrawing ranibizumab PDS finished product from the vial, the filter needle was removed and syringes were then closed by means of a sterile closing cone made of PE or PP. The syringes were held for 4 hours at ambient temperature and ambient lighting conditions. The results demonstrate that ranibizumab PDS finished product is physically and chemically stable under the tested conditions) when stored in PP or PC syringes. No impact to product quality was observed when comparing control samples and the in-use stability test samples. From a microbiological point of view, the ranibizumab finished product solution should be used immediately. If not used immediately, the product should be used as soon as possible and should not exceed 4 h at ambient temperature to allow for typical pharmacy handling.

## 3.3.2. Manufacture of the product and process controls

## Manufacture

## Description of manufacturing process and process controls

Ranibizumab PDS finished product is manufactured and tested at Vetter Pharma-Fertigung GmbH &amp; Co. KG, Langenargen, Germany (VLA), in cleanroom and associated areas. Manufacturing sites responsible for manufacturing and testing of ranibizumab PDS finished product operate in accordance with EU GMP.

Ranibizumab for port delivery system is manufactured as a liquid dosage form in vials designed to deliver a nominal fill volume (labelled net quantity) of 0.1 mL. The finished product manufacturing process has been appropriately described. It comprises thawing of active substance at ambient temperature, homogenisation, pooling and mixing, bioburden reduction filtration, in-line sterile filtration, aseptic filling into glass vials, stoppering, capping and crimping Acceptable quality limit (AQL) testing is conducted. The vials are transported from the finished product manufacturing site to the labelling and secondary packaging site using qualified shipping systems to maintain the finished product temperature.

The ranibizumab PDS finished product manufacturing process is a standard manufacturing process (filland-finish) for MAbs and has been adequately described. Hold times and process parameters are indicated. IPCs are depicted in section P.3.4.

Only one refiltration is permitted for the ranibizumab PDS finished product manufacturing process at the bioburden reduction filtration step prior to in-line sterile filtration due to failure of filter integrity testing or technical problems resulting in exceeded hold times. Refiltration is strictly not allowed for addressing microbial contamination or resolving other product-quality issues In relation to the product quality impact of refiltration, the Applicant is recommended to provide the analytical validation results (REC).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The approach is considered adequate.

## Control of critical steps and intermediates

The quality of ranibizumab PDS finished product is controlled by IPCs during critical steps of manufacture. IPCs are in place for microbial control, the fill volume and protein concentration. Bioburden at the sterile filtration step is controlled via acceptance criterion

Depending on criticality, IPCs are tested against defined action limits or acceptance criteria. Both action limits and acceptance criteria and the consequences/actions taken in case of not meeting an action limit or acceptance criterion have been explained. A confirmed failure in meeting an acceptance criterion will lead to batch rejection. Filter integrity testing after in-line sterile filtration has been reclassified as an in-process test with acceptance criteria.

A description of analytical procedures that are specific to finished product in-process control testing (bioburden) and their validation is provided.

An AQL approach has been implemented after the final filled vial visual inspection as a measure to control the quality of the inspection process and the acceptability of the inspected finished product. AQL testing is conducted on the inspected batch to assess the outcome of the inspection process and to ensure that vials with defects (including visible particles and defects in caps or crimping) have been successfully removed.

There are no intermediates isolated in the manufacture of ranibizumab PDS finished product.

## Process validation

Validation of the finished product manufacturing process included the manufacture of consecutive PPQ batches The finished product PPQ batches were manufactured using eight independent active substance batches. The PPQ campaign was supplemented by additional GMP batches used to validate the manufacturing process of the ranibizumab PDS finished product in the PFS configuration. The processes between the PPQ batches and the GMP batches are identical prior to the step of in-line sterile filtration. Data from the PPQ batches and the GMP batches confirm that the process is validated and capable of producing consistent product quality at scale in the commercial manufacturing site. All CQAs met their acceptance criteria, all IPCs met their acceptance criteria or action limits, all CPPs and non-CPPs were maintained within their acceptable ranges (except for planned challenge conditions), and process performance was consistent among the PPQ batches. Challenge was conducted for the additional GMP batches, only.

Process design studies have been conducted to support the evaluation of acceptable ranges, identification of critical process parameters, and the establishment of process and hold times for the finished product manufacturing process. Process parameters were assessed for impact on product quality to define critical process parameter and process parameter acceptable ranges. A RRF assessment was completed to identify potential critical process parameter based on the potential impact to critical quality attributes and process performance. Relevant CQAs for the finished product process were identified for assessment in process design studies. Process design studies were performed to evaluate the impact on pCPPs on relevant CQAs. Process parameters were classified as critical process parameters or non-critical process parameters based on the observed impact on relevant critical quality attributes and process performance in the process design studies and acceptable ranges were defined. Process and hold times were defined considering impacts on product quality and microbial control during finished product manufacturing.

In summary, the approach used for identification of CPPs and the establishment of their acceptable ranges is agreed.

Microbial hold studies were conducted covering the thawing and homogenisation time and the hold time of finished product solution in the filtration vessel.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

After the vials are labelled and packaged, the finished product is shipped by road and/or air to distribution centres. Different active and passive systems are used for shipment.

## Control of excipients

All excipients are compendial (Ph. Eur.) and comply with the applicable requirements.

## 3.3.3. Product specification

## Specifications

Specification for ranibizumab PDS finished product include control of identity, purity and impurities, potency and other general tests.

The release specification for ranibizumab PDS finished product include tests for appearance, identity, purity and impurities, content of protein, potency, general attributes, and microbial safety. Ranibizumab PDS is formulated with polysorbate 20The polysorbate 20 concentration is tested both at finished product release and stability. The stability specification for ranibizumab PDS finished product comprises a reduced set of parameters: physical state, volume in container, osmolality, identity, protein content, sterility and bacterial endotoxins are not monitored. Instead of sterility, container closure integrity is monitored at stability testing. Since no change in these parameters is expected, this is acceptable. The panel of quality attributes proposed for release and stability testing of ranibizumab PDS finished product is in general adequate and in line with ICH Q6B and EMA/CHMP/BWP/532517/2008 guidelines, and Ph. Eur. 2031.

## Justification of specifications

## Method development

The development of analytical procedures for ranibizumab PDS involved several changes and improvements throughout the different development phasesprocesses.

## Justification of specifications

The acceptance criteria were established taking the following considerations into account: clinical experience, stability and process effects, product-specific knowledge, prior knowledge, current compendia or regulatory guidelines, and formulation development studies. Shelf-life setting involves working backward from the end-of-shelf-life (EOSL) criterion to set appropriate release and stability testing criteria to ensure the finished product remains within acceptable limits throughout its shelf life.

The specifications are deemed sufficient to control the quality of ranibizumab finished product. in line with Annex 1 of Directive 2001/83 (see Directive 2003/63 EUR-Lex - 32003L0063 - EN) stating that 'Unless there is appropriate justification, the maximum acceptable deviation in the active substance content of the finished product shall not exceed +- 5 % at the time of manufacture.'

The simulated in-use stability study has also been conducted with aged implant and aged ranibizumab finished product. Functionality of the quasi drug-device-combination was demonstrated.

## Analytical procedures

Descriptions of the compendial and in-house analytical methods are sufficiently detailed and acceptable. For the compendial methods (colour, clarity/opalescence, pH, visible and sub-visible particles, bacterial endotoxins and sterility) reference has been made to the respective Ph. Eur. monographs. For both the compendial and the internal methods, a reference to the respective in-house SOP and a brief description of the method has been provided.

All analytical methods used for release testing of the finished product have been appropriately validated based on the principles provided in ICH Q2 (R1) guideline. Established pharmacopoeial analytical procedures, including those for colour, clarity/opalescence, pH, visible and sub-visible particles, bacterial endotoxins and sterility are performed in accordance with the specified compendial method and have not been validated. The methods have been verified. The analytical procedures used for bacterial endotoxin, sterility, and container closure integrity test have been validated for the ranibizumab finished product to determine their suitability.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The presented validations for analytical methods are acceptable and demonstrate the suitability of the analytical procedures for their intended use.

## Batch analysis

Batch release data are presented for PPQ finished product batches manufactured according to the intended commercial manufacturing process

Overall, the batch release data shows consistent and comparable quality of ranibizumab PDS active substance manufactured across all batches of process All the active substance batches comply with the pre-established specifications valid at the time of testing.

In summary, a considerable amount of batch release data is available which indicates that the process is under control and able to consistently deliver finished product meeting predefined quality requirements.

## Reference standard

See active substance part.

## Characterisation of impurities

No new impurities have been introduced during the finished product manufacturing process; reference is made to section 3.2.S.3.2 of the dossier. This is acceptable. Furthermore, elemental impurities have been addressed in this sections, extractables/leachables have been addressed in section 3.2.P.2 and nitrosamines have been addressed in module 1 of the dossier.

A risk assessment for elemental impurities (all considered not intentionally added) has been conducted and no risk was identified. To confirm the predicted absence of risks associated with elemental impurities incorporation into the finished product, one finished product batch was tested for the elements using ICP-MS. All results were below the control threshold for injection products.

Extractables and leachables studies were performed for the type I glass vial and the fluororesin-coated rubber stopper. Leachables were below the safety concern threshold limit for the compounds or were not detectable.

A short summary of the conducted nitrosamine risk assessment was provided in a separate document in Module 1 (Section 1.6.1 'Nitrosamines Annex'). Ranibizumab PDS was assessed for the risk of nitrosamine impurities according to the principles outlined in CHMP Article 5[3] Opinion EMEA-H-A5[3]- 1490 and the revised EMA Questions and Answers for Marketing Authorization Holders on the CHMP Opinion for the Article 5[3] of Regulation [EC] No 726/2004 Referral on Nitrosamine Impurities in Human Medicinal Products.

A brief summary of the performed risk assessment for presence of N-nitrosamine has been provided. It is agreed that no risk for the contamination of the finished product with N-nitrosamines or for the formation of N-nitrosamines during manufacture, shelf life or the in-use period due to the presence of nitrosating agents has been identified.

## 3.3.4. Stability of the product

The proposed long-term storage condition for ranibizumab PDS finished product is 2-8°C, protected from light. A shelf-life of 36 months is proposed, based on long-term primary stability data (real-time recommended storage conditions) generated from clinical batches and process performance qualification (PPQ) batches. e. All batches were stored in glass vials representative of the commercial container closure system.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Long-term stability data for clinical batches are available for 48 months and 36 months Stability data at accelerated conditions (25°C) are available for 6 months.The clinical batches can be regarded representative of the commercial process. Long-term (2-8°C) stability data for PPQ batches are available for 36 months.

The available stability data for samples stored at the recommended storage conditions (2-8°C) show good stability with no (significant) trends observed. The same stability behaviour is observed for the representative clinical batches and the PPQ batches.

A shelf-life of 36 months at the recommended storage conditions is claimed. The presented data for ranibizumab PDS finished product manufactured at the commercial facility support the intended shelflife.

To further support the shelf-life, a temperature cycling study has been initiated on finished product PPQ batch to support potential temperature excursions/time out of recommended storage condition relevant for manufacturing, shipping, and handling of finished product vials. All current results are within specifications.

Photostability: Stability of the finished product when exposed to light was investigated for PPQ batch Samples were exposed to conditions consistent with ICH Q1BIt has been shown that ranibizumab PDS finished product is light sensitive. The packaging configuration adequately protects the finished product vials from light. The product label contains a note to store the product in the outer carton to protect from light.

A post-approval stability commitment has been provided and is considered acceptable. Stabilityindicating parameters are covered.

## 3.3.5. Medical device aspects

The Contivue PDS to be used with Susvimo (provided separately) is a novel drug delivery system that consists of an intraocular implant, a customised formulation of ranibizumab for PDS (100 mg/mL), and four ancillary devices used to fill, insert, refill, and explant the implant: an initial fill needle [IFN], an insertion tool assembly [ITA], a refill needle [RFN], and an explant tool [ET], respectively (Figure 2). The PDS implant is a refillable, permanent intraocular device uniquely designed for the continuous delivery of ranibizumab (100 mg/mL).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 1: Device constituents of PDS

<!-- image -->

Note: Images of devices not to scale.

The mechanism of finished product release from the implant is passive, concentration gradient-driven two-way diffusion. This process is described mathematically using Fick's first law of diffusion, which relates the mass flux of the drug to its concentration gradient and diffusion coefficient.

Device descriptions, principles of operation, packaging description, a summary of safety and performance testing, information on design validation and the manufacturing process and controls is provided in the dossier. Within the scope of the design verification studies, finished product compatibility with the IFN and RFN has been shown.

Some design changes were implemented after the phase III pivotal clinical study to address septum dislodgement and improve robustness (

Since the design changes have no direct impact on the drug release from the implant, the design verification studies conducted before the design changes remain valid. Design verification testing covering the shelf life of the PDS devices has demonstrated their adequacy of performance. Ranibizumab PDS finished product compatibility with the IFN and RFN were validated. RFN compatibility was evaluated using accelerated-aged needles, which represent worst-case conditions The Applicant is recommended to provide the results of the ongoing real-time aging study for the siliconised RFN, once available (REC).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Drug release from the implant was evaluated in several studies.

Characterisation of ranibizumab was performed during in vitro release studies to assess the stability of the drug under in vitro drug release conditions.

Since ranibizumab is light sensitive, and it stays in the implant and/or eye up to 6 months, photostability testing was performed.

A stability study was also conducted for ranibizumab in vitreous humour.

A ranibizumab-specific extractables/leachables study was performed as part of the biocompatibility assessment of the implant.

Since ranibizumab PDS can only be used in combination with the Contivue, considering the criticality of the devices for the safe and effective use of Susvimo, CE certificates and Declarations of conformity were provided.

Considering the totality of the data provided, the use of Contivue PDS with Susvimo is acceptable.

Two additional general medical devices, required for initial fill and implant procedure and implant refill exchange procedure, are mentioned in the product information: a 1 mL Luer Lock Tip syringe and transfer needle (19-gauge, 1½ inch). No issues are raised on these named devices.

## 3.3.6. Adventitious agents

The fermentation process of ranibizumab PDS is in a serum-free medium. The only animal-derived materials added during fermentation are tryptone and N-Z-amine, type A. This minimises a possible contamination for adventitious viruses. The cells used for production of ranibizumab PDS are of bacterial origin (E. coli), therefore no virus safety testing on cell banks and unprocessed bulk has been performed.

E. coli cell banks and fermentation broth are tested for the absence of non-host contamination and bacteriophages.

For active substance, bioburden and endotoxin tests are performed throughout the purification process and release; and for the finished product, in-process testing of bioburden and other measures are conducted to ensure microbial safety during the finished product manufacturing process, as well as endotoxin and sterility testing at finished product release.

In summary, the adventitious agents safety of ranibizumab PDS has been sufficiently demonstrated.

## 3.4. Discussion and conclusions on quality aspects

Information on development, manufacture and control of the active substance and finished product have been presented in a satisfactory manner. The results of tests carried out indicate satisfactory consistency and uniformity of important product quality characteristics, and these in turn lead to the conclusion that the product should have a satisfactory and uniform performance in the clinic.

From a quality perspective, the marketing authorisation application for Susvimo is approvable.

## 3.5. Recommendation(s) for future quality development

1. The Applicant is recommended to provide the long-term stability data up to 60 months for the active substance X0SP PPQ batch 3466821, once available.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

2. In relation to the product quality impact of refiltration, the Applicant is recommended to provide the analytical validation results, once available.
3. The Applicant is recommended to provide the results of the ongoing real-time aging study for the siliconised RFN, once available.

## 4. Non-clinical aspects

## 4.1. Introduction

The Port Delivery System with ranibizumab (PDS) is a novel treatment option intended to be used in patients with nAMD, a chronic retinal disease, that still remains an unmet medical need despite available therapies (see above). The PDS is a refillable, permanent intraocular implant designed for the continuous delivery of ranibizumab. It is designed to maintain therapeutic drug concentrations in the vitreous for longer durations than the available anti-VEGF treatments administered by IVT injection, potentially reducing the number of required visits and treatments.
