## Concomitant and rescue therapies

## Permitted therapies

Patients were permitted to use the following therapies during the study:

- IVT administration of FDA-approved anti-VEGF agents, including Sponsor-provided ranibizumab 0.5 mg, at the discretion of the evaluating investigator in the fellow eye for nAMD
- Continuous use of aspirin or nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID) treatment (these treatments were interrupted prior to implant surgery)
- Ongoing anticoagulant or anti-platelet therapy (other than aspirin or other NSAIDs) (these treatments were interrupted prior to implant surgery)
- Cataract surgery in the study eye, if clinically indicated and occurring 7 or more days after the last study treatment, with the next study treatment held for 7 or more days following the surgery
- Treatment, as clinically indicated, for the onset of increased IOP and/or glaucoma in the study eye during a patient's study participation

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Use of topical steroids post-implant insertion or post-implant removal surgery in the study eye
- Conditional use of magnetic resonance imaging scans for patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm (for additional information refer to the current PDS

## Prohibited therapies

- Concurrent use of any systemic anti-VEGF agents
- Concurrent study eye treatment for nAMD with anti-VEGF agents other than the study-assigned treatment
- Concurrent fellow eye treatment for nAMD with unapproved anti-VEGF therapy
- Concurrent treatment with laser photocoagulation (any type) for nAMD in the study eye
- Concurrent treatment with Visudyne® for nAMD in study eye
- Concurrent use of IVT corticosteroids in the study eye
- Concurrent use of subtenon corticosteroids in the study eye (except at the conclusion of PDS implantation or implant removal surgery)
- Concurrent use of and participation in other experimental therapies (except those with minerals and vitamins)

## Randomisation and Blinding

Patients were planned to be randomized in a 3:2 allocation to one of the two treatment groups stratified by BCVA score (&lt;74 letters vs. ≥ 74 letters) at the day of the patient's randomization visit. Randomisation was planned to b performed through an IxRS.

This was an open-label study. Patients and study site personnel were not masked with regard to patient assignment because of difficulties of maintaining masking following the surgical procedure. Additional safety visits were planned only to the implant arms, and implant visualisation occurred during ophthalmic examinations.

The Sponsor explains that at least the visual acuity examiner (VAE) was masked to the treatment as best as possible to patient study eye assignment, study visit eye and patient treatment assignment. The VAE had no access to patient's BCVA scores from previous visits, (s)he provided no direct or indirect patient care, and patients and unmasked personnel were asked not to discuss the study eye assignment.

## Patient population

Adult patients with CNV secondary to AMD, diagnosed within 9 months prior to screening and treated with at least 3 injections with any anti-VEGF agent within the last 6 months prior to screening, were planned to be enrolled in this study. This approach aimed to limit the number of patients who would potentially not respond to continuous delivery of ranibizumab, thus minimizing the chance for an individual anti-VEGF non-responder to undergo surgery and receive the PDS implant.

## Inclusion criteria

## General Inclusion Criteria

Patients had to meet the following general inclusion criteria at screening and randomization for study entry:

- Ability and willingness to provide signed informed consent
- Age ≥50 years, at time of signing Informed Consent Form
- Ability and willingness to undertake all scheduled visits and assessments
- For women of childbearing potential: agreement to remain abstinent (refrain from heterosexual intercourse) or use contraceptive measures

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Ocular Inclusion Criteria

Patients had to meet the following ocular inclusion criteria for the study eye for study entry:

- Initial diagnosis of exudative nAMD within 9 months prior to the screening visit
- Previous treatment with at least 3 anti-VEGF IVT injections (e.g., ranibizumab, bevacizumab, or aflibercept) for nAMD per standard of care within 6 months prior to the screening visit

If a patient had not received at least 3 anti-VEGF injections as described above but was otherwise eligible for the study, the patient could be treated in a run-in phase to meet this specific criterion.

- Demonstrated response to prior anti-VEGF IVT treatment since diagnosis, as evidenced at screening by the following:

Overall decrease in nAMD disease activity detected on SD-OCT, as assessed by the investigator and confirmed by the central reading centre

and

Stable or improved BCVA

- BCVA of 34 letters or better (20/200 or better approximate Snellen equivalent), using ETDRS chart at a starting distance of 4 meters at screening and randomization visits
- All subtypes of nAMD lesions were permissible (i.e., type I, type II, type III, or mixed forms per OCT classification); nAMD lesions at the time of diagnosis had to involve the macula (6-mm diameter centred at the fovea).
- Sufficiently clear ocular media and adequate pupillary dilation to allow for analysis and grading by the central reading centre of FP, FA, FAF, and SD-OCT images

## Exclusion Criteria

## Prior Ocular Treatment

Patients who met any of the following prior ocular treatment exclusion criteria were excluded from study entry.

## Study Eye

- History of vitrectomy surgery, submacular surgery, or other surgical intervention for AMD
- Prior treatment with Visudyne®, external-beam radiation therapy, or transpupillary thermotherapy
- Previous treatment with corticosteroid IVT injection
- Previous intraocular device implantation
- Previous laser (any type) used for AMD treatment

## Either Eye

- Treatment with anti-VEGF agents other than ranibizumab within 1 month prior to the randomization visit
- Prior participation in a clinical trial involving anti-VEGF drugs within 6 months prior to the randomization visit, other than ranibizumab

## CNV Lesion Characteristics

Patients who met any of the following exclusion criteria related to CNV lesion characteristics were excluded from study entry.

## Study Eye

- Subretinal haemorrhage that involved the centre of the fovea, if the haemorrhage was greater than 0.5 disc area (1.27 mm 2 ) in size at screening
- Subfoveal fibrosis or subfoveal atrophy

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Either Eye

- CNV due to other causes, such as ocular histoplasmosis, trauma, or pathologic myopia

## Concurrent Ocular Conditions

Patients who met any of the following exclusion criteria related to concurrent ocular conditions were excluded from study entry.

## Study Eye

- Retinal pigment epithelial tear
- Any concurrent intraocular condition (e.g., cataract, glaucoma, diabetic retinopathy, or epiretinal membrane) that either could require surgical intervention during the study to prevent or treat visual loss that might result from that condition or could affect interpretation of study results
- Active intraocular inflammation (grade trace or above)
- History of vitreous haemorrhage
- History of rhegmatogenous retinal detachment
- History of rhegmatogenous retinal tears or peripheral retinal breaks within 3 months prior to the randomization visit
- Aphakia or absence of the posterior capsule

Previous violation of the posterior capsule was also an exclusion criterion unless it occurred as a result of yttrium-aluminium garnet (YAG) laser posterior capsulotomy in association with prior, posterior chamber intraocular lens implantation.

- Spherical equivalent of the refractive error demonstrating more than 8 diopters of myopia
- Preoperative refractive error that exceeded 8 diopters of myopia, for patients who had undergone prior refractive or cataract surgery in the study eye
- Intraocular surgery (including cataract surgery) within 3 months preceding the randomization visit
- Uncontrolled ocular hypertension or glaucoma (defined as intraocular pressure [IOP]
- 25 mmHg or a cup to disc ratio &gt; 0.8, despite treatment with antiglaucoma medication) and any such condition the investigator determined could require a glaucoma-filtering surgery during a patient's participation in the study
- History of glaucoma-filtering surgery, tube shunts, or microinvasive glaucoma surgery
- History of corneal transplant
- History of prior vitrectomy surgery and absence of posterior capsule

## Either Eye

- History of idiopathic or autoimmune-associated uveitis
- Active infectious conjunctivitis, keratitis, scleritis, or endophthalmitis

## Concurrent Systemic Conditions

Patients who met any of the following exclusion criteria related to concurrent systemic conditions were excluded from study entry:

- Inability to comply with study schedule or procedures as described in the study protocol
- Uncontrolled blood pressure (defined as systolic blood pressure &gt; 180 mmHg and/or diastolic blood pressure &gt; 110 mmHg, while a patient was at rest)

If a patient's initial measurement exceeded these values, a second reading could be taken 30 or more minutes later. If the patient's blood pressure required antihypertensive medication, the patient could become eligible if medication was taken continuously for at least 30 days prior to Day 1.

- History of stroke within the last 3 months prior to informed consent
- Uncontrolled atrial fibrillation within 3 months of informed consent
- History of myocardial infarction within the last 3 months prior to informed consent

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- History of other disease, metabolic dysfunction, or clinical laboratory finding giving reasonable suspicion of a disease or condition that contraindicated the use of ranibizumab or placement of the implant and that could affect interpretation of the results of the study or rendered the patient at high risk of treatment complications in the opinion of the investigator
- Current systemic treatment for a confirmed active systemic infection
- Use of any systemic anti-VEGF agents
- Chronic use of oral corticosteroids (&gt; 10 mg/day of prednisone or equivalent)
- Active cancer within 12 months of randomization except for appropriately treated carcinoma in situ of the cervix, non-melanoma skin carcinoma, and prostate cancer with a Gleason score of &lt; 6 and a stable prostate-specific antigen for &gt; 12 months
- Previous participation in any non-ocular (systemic) disease studies of investigational drugs within 1 month preceding the informed consent (excluding vitamins and minerals)
- Use of antimitotic or antimetabolite therapy within 30 days or 5 elimination half-lives of the randomization visit
- Requirement for continuous use of any medications or treatments indicated in Section 'Prohibited Therapy'
- Pregnant or breastfeeding, or intending to become pregnant during the treatment period and for at least 28 days after the last IVT injection of ranibizumab or 1 year after the last implant refillexchange of ranibizumab

## 5.3.2.1.3. Objectives and estimands

## Primary objective

The primary objective of Study GR40548 (Archway) was to evaluate the non-inferiority and equivalence in efficacy of ranibizumab delivered via the PDS filled with the 100 mg/mL formulation compared to that of 10 mg/mL (0.5 mg dose) ranibizumab Q4W via IVT injections. The primary efficacy endpoint was the change in BCVA from baseline averaged over Weeks 36 and 40. The noninferiority margin was 4.5 letters, and the equivalence margin was ±4.5 letters.

## Estimand for the primary objective

The clinical question of interest was to determine whether ranibizumab delivered via the PDS filled with the 100 mg/mL formulation was non-inferior and subsequently equivalent to 10 mg/mL (0.5 mg dose) ranibizumab Q4W via IVT injections with regards to the change in BCVA from baseline averaged over Weeks 36 and 40 regardless of whether a patient experienced any of the intercurrent events prior to Week 40 (i.e. using a treatment policy strategy).

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on primary estimand

## Analysis set

The Efficacy population was defined as all patients who were randomised and received the study treatment. Patients were analysed according to treatment actually received. The Efficacy population was the primary efficacy population for the primary efficacy analysis.

The per-protocol population was defined as all patients in the Efficacy population who do not have a major protocol deviation that impact the efficacy evaluation. Patients were analysed according to treatment actually received. The per-protocol population was planned to be used for the sensitivity analysis for the primary endpoint and the key secondary endpoint.

## Main analysis method

The primary analysis was performed using the mixed-effect model with repeated measures (MMRM) based on all available data up to Week 40. All observed measurements were included in the analysis regardless whether or not a patient has an intercurrent event. Missing data were implicitly imputed by the MMRM model, assuming a missing at random mechanism.

The dependent variable in the MMRM model was the change from baseline in BCVA score at post-baseline visits, up to 40 weeks, and the independent variables were the treatment group, time, treatment-by-time interaction, baseline BCVA score (continuous), and the randomization stratification factor of baseline BCVA (&lt; 74 letters vs. ≥ 74 letters) fixed effects. The MMRM model assumed an unstructured covariance structure.

For the primary efficacy endpoint, if a lower bound of a two-sided 95.03% CI for the difference of two treatments was greater than -4.5 letters (the NI margin), then treatment via PDS was considered noninferior to monthly IVT ranibizumab treatment. If the two-sided 95.03% CI was within - 4.5L and +4.5L, then the two treatment regimens were considered clinically equivalent.

## Multiplicity control

The two primary objectives of non-inferiority and equivalence between the two treatment groups were tested controlling the overall type I error by a fixed sequence testing procedure (Westfall and Krishen,

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

2001): If the PDS 100mg/mL arm is shown to be non-inferior to the IVT arm at the one-sided 0.02485 level, then the equivalence test was conducted using two one-sided 0.02485 tests. The alpha level was adjusted to 0.02485 (one-sided) due to an interim safety monitoring by the iDMC.

The key secondary endpoints were only defined post-hoc after an EMA scientific advice. A fixed sequence testing procedure was used. The NI tests for the key secondary endpoints were performed in the order shown above with a NI margin of -3.9 letters, proceeding sequentially, and testing each after achieving NI on the previous endpoint.

## Missing data

All observed measurements were included in the primary analysis regardless whether or not a patient has an intercurrent event. Missing values were implicitly imputed by the MMRM model, assuming a missing at random mechanism.

## Sensitivity and supplementary analysis

As a sensitivity analysis the per-protocol analysis following the same methods as the primary analysis except the Per-Protocol Population was used. Further sensitivity analyses were the trimmed mean analysis, and analyses using different handling rules for intercurrent events.

## Secondary objectives

The specific efficacy objectives and corresponding endpoints for the study are outlined in the Table below (Table 2 from the SCE).

Key secondary endpoints, which had been recommended by EMA during prior SA procedure, were NI of the PDS arm to the IVT ranibizumab arm with regard to 1) the change in BCVA score from baseline averaged over Week 36 and Week 40, and 2) the change in BCVA score from baseline averaged over Week 44 and Week 48, both with a NI margin of 3.9 letters.

Consequently, the following endpoints were proposed as key secondary endpoints:

- Change from baseline in BCVA score averaged over Week 36 and Week 40
- Change from baseline in BCVA score averaged over Week 44 and Week 48
- Change from baseline in BCVA score averaged over Week 88 and Week 92

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on the key secondary estimand

Analysis of these key secondary endpoints followed the same method as the analysis of the primary endpoint in all respects except for the NI margin and timepoints included in the analysis.

For binary secondary efficacy endpoints, the proportion of patients in each treatment group and the overall difference in proportions between treatment groups were estimated using the weighted average of the observed proportions and the differences in observed proportions over the strata defined by randomization stratification factor of baseline BCVA (&lt;74 letters vs. ≥74 letters) using the Cochran -Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) weights (Cochran 1954; Mantel and Haenszel 1959). All observed measurements were included in the analysis regardless whether or not a patient has an intercurrent event.

## 5.3.2.1.4. Results

## Participant flow and numbers analysed

## Disposition of patients

A total of 619 patients were screened. Of these, 201 were considered screen failures (the main reasons for screen failure were lack of response to anti-VEGF treatment during run-in phase for patients who had not received at least 3 prior anti-VEGF treatments [n=50], subfoveal fibrosis or atrophy [n=35] and lack of ability and willingness to undertake all scheduled visits and assessments [n=18]).

Therefore, 418 patients were enrolled and randomized (251 to the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 167 to the IVT arm) (Figure 2). Due to a high speed of enrolment, combined with a lower screen failure rate than expected, the number of patients enrolled exceeded the number required according to the sample size calculation (n=360). A summary of the number of patients enrolled by 78 sites that are participating in the study has been provided in the CSR.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Source: t\\_ds\\_w96\\_EP\\_FCSR.

Three patients randomized to the PDS 100 mg/mL arm were never treated (one patient was unable to attend the Day 1 visit in the stipulated timeframe, one experienced atrial fibrillation and was unable to comply with study visits, and one did not have nAMD diagnosis within 9 months). The Efficacy population (i.e. randomized and treated) therefore comprised 415 patients (248 in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 167 in the IVT arm).

Study completion was overall high: through Week 96, 14 patients (14/248 [5.6%]) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 13 patients (13/167 [7.8%]) in the IVT arm discontinued the study. . No patients continued in Study GR40548 past Week 120. Through Week 96, patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm had received an initial fill and a mean of 2.9 refills while patients in the IVT ranibizumab arm had received a mean of 22.9 treatments out of a possible 25 treatments.

The most common reason for study discontinuation in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm was death (8/248 [3.2%]) while the most common reason in the IVT arm was withdrawal by patient (7/167 [4.2%]).

Treatment discontinuations are presented below.

Twelve patients died on study (8 in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 4 patients in the IVT arm) prior to the end of the study. One additional patient in the IVT arm withdrew from the study at Day 187 and later died of pancreatic carcinoma.

The overall time on study was balanced across treatment arms (mean 96.2 weeks in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and mean 94.6 weeks in the IVT arm through the entire study).

Premature withdrawal from study treatment A total of 24 patients (24/248 [9.7%]) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 13 patients (13/167 [7.8%]) in the IVT arm discontinued treatment early and did not complete the study treatment (Table 2). The main reasons for discontinuation of treatment were AEs in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and withdrawal by subject in the IVT arm.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A total of 210 patients (210/248 [84.7%]) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 110 patients (110/167 [65.9%]) in the IVT arm had completed study treatment at Week 96. In the PDS 100 mg/mL arm, patients discontinued treatment due to AEs (10/248 [4.0%]), death (7/248 [2.8%]), lost to follow-up (3/248 [1.2%]), other reasons (2/248 [0.8%]), non-compliance with study drug (1/248 [0.4%]), and withdrawal by subject (1/248 [0.4%]). In the IVT arm, patients discontinued treatment due to withdrawal by subject (7/167 [4.2%]), death (4/167 [2.4%]), AE (1/167 [0.6%]), and physician decision (1/167 [0.6%]).

At the end of the study, one patient who had discontinued treatment due to an AE later died (see Safety Section).

Nine of the patients who discontinued treatment and did not complete the study had their implants removed (6 discontinuations due to implant dislocation/breakage, 1 discontinuation due to Lucentis allergy, 1 discontinuation due to endophthalmitis, and 1 discontinuation due to conjunctival retraction).

## Deviations from study plan

## Protocol Amendments

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Protocol Version 1 was finalized on 31 May 2018 and was amended on 29 July 2019 (Amendment 1, Version 2), on 18 December 2019 (Amendment 2, Version 3), on 16 June 2020 (Amendment 3, Version 4), and on 02 February 2021 (Amendment 4, Version 5). All versions of the protocol are presented.

## Key changes incorporated in Protocol Version 2:

- Adjustment made throughout the protocol from 'rescue' to 'supplemental' injections in order to distinguish between PDS with ranibizumab patients who receive IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg in the study eye as allowed per protocol Section 5.1.5.3 from PDS patients who receive prohibited antiVEGF treatment in the study eye that is not covered by protocol Section 5.1.5.3.
- The objective evaluating patient preference per the PDS Patient Preference Questionnaire (PPPQ) was moved from the secondary efficacy objectives to the exploratory patient experience objectives (Section 2).
- The central subfield thickness abbreviation has been updated from CSFT to CST to align with Roche global ophthalmology programs and with the reading centre standards.
- Key changes incorporated in Protocol Version 3:
- At selected sites, an additional PK sample will be collected from patients in the IVT arm 1-5 days after an IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg injection in order to collect a sample near Cmax (Section 3.5.4, Appendix 2)
- The secondary endpoints of proportion of patients who lose &lt; 15 letters in best corrected visual acuity (BCVA) from baseline over week 36 and week 40 and overtime were removed (Section 2, Table 1).
- Key changes incorporated in Protocol Version 4:
- Several alternate final study visit scenarios have been provided to allow patients to remain in the study and receive treatment at regular intervals until consenting patients can enrol into an openlabel safety extension study per-protocol (Section 3.1, Figure 1, Appendices 1 and 2).
- Schedules of activities and assessment timepoints have been provided for these alternate final study visit scenarios (Appendix 1, Table 3 and Appendix 2, Table 3).
- Key changes incorporated in Protocol Version 5:
- 'Device dislocation' has been added as an ocular AESI. Furthermore, 'Device dislocation' has been added to the list of anticipated risks associated with the PDS implant and/or implant insertion procedure (Section 5.1.3.7). In addition, language updates have been made to the other anticipated risks (vitreous haemorrhage, conjunctival bleb, conjunctival erosion, conjunctival retraction, rhegmatogenous retinal detachment, endophthalmitis)
- Administration schedule for self-administered antimicrobial and/or anti-inflammatory ophthalmic drops before and after the Port Delivery System with ranibizumab (PDS) procedures have been updated).
- Study treatment dose interruption criteria for local or systemic infection have been updated (Section 5.1.5.2, Table 2).
- The statistical method to evaluate the secondary efficacy objective \"proportion of patients who undergo supplemental treatment\" was modified in order to more accurately reflect the number of patients receiving supplemental treatment among those able to be assessed for the need for supplemental treatment (Section 6.4.2).
- Instructions on the management of device dislocation have been added (Section 5.1.5.8, Table 7) and recommended management of patients with vitreous haemorrhage, conjunctival retraction or

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

conjunctival erosion, conjunctival bleb, endophthalmitis instructions have been updated (Sections 5.1.5.4 to 5.1.5.7).

## Visual Acuity Examiners (VAE) Masking

In the study, the visual acuity examiners (VAEs) were to remain masked to study eye, treatment arm, and study visit type. In November 2019, the Sponsor identified that, due to a limitation within the Clinical Trial Management System (CTMS), 144 VAEs at 66 study sites were listed under the category of the Study Coordinator Role and therefore were inadvertently registered to receive automated emails from the portal used to share study information with site staff (DrugDev). These automated emails were intended to remind Study Coordinators of upcoming patient visits. Within the subject line, information of the patient number, study visit week and type was included, which could indirectly inform the patient's treatment arm and study visit type to the masked VAEs (assigned to 232 patients total).

Immediately upon identification of the issue, the email notifications were discontinued until the roles within the CTMS system were reviewed and updated to accurately reflect user roles. All other automated systems used in the study were also evaluated, to ensure no similar issues had occurred. These CTMS user access checks are now in place and occur monthly.

During general conduct of the trial, sites and VAEs were instructed to self-report in the event a VAE was unmasked. As of CCOD none of the VAEs self-reported having been unmasked due to receiving these automated emails. Other reasons for potential unmasking of VAEs were reported as major protocol deviations.

The Sponsor has met with external consulting bodies including the Executive Advisory Committee (this committee is comprised of scientific experts who play an advisory role for the PDS clinical development program) and the iDMC to discuss this GCP breach. The Roche GCP Council was also consulted and concurred with the actions taken by the study team. The Executive Advisory Committee and iDMC agreed that the likelihood of data integrity being affected as a result of this issue is considered to be low. This is recognized. However, to further clarify that there is no critical issue with regard to data integrity resulting from the erroneously sent automated emails, the applicant performed descriptive analyses to explore whether data would suggest any biases resulting from this breach. Mean BCVA and mean change from baseline in BCVA were summarized according to treatment group and whether or not the VAE received an email at Week 16, Week 20, and Week 40 visits. Analysis of the results did not suggest biases that would likely impact the efficacy assessments.

## Baseline data

Baseline demographics were generally balanced across treatment groups (see Table below). The mean age at randomization was 75.0 years (75.2 years in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 74.8 years in the IVT arm) and 56.4% were aged ≥75 years (56.9% in the PDS 100 mg /mL arm and 55.7% in the IVT arm). The majority of patients were female (59.0% among all patients; 58.5% in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 59.9% in the IVT arm) and white (96.6% among all patients; 96.8% in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 96.4% in the IVT arm).

Baseline ocular characteristics for the study eye were generally balanced across treatment groups. Among all patients, the mean baseline ETDRS BCVA was 74.8 letters (74.4 letters in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 75.5 letters in the IVT arm), that is an approximate Snellen equivalent of 20/32, and 66.5% of patients had a score of 74 letters or better (65.7% in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 67.7% in the IVT arm). Mean baseline CPT was 177.0 microns (176.9 microns in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 177.2 microns in the IVT arm), and the mean baseline CST was 307.9 microns (312.7 microns in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 300.9 microns in the IVT arm). The mean number of anti-VEGF injections prior to first study treatment in both treatment arms was 5.0. Of note, in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm, 1 patient had received 31 prior injections. Time since first diagnosis of nAMD in this patient was 152 months, in violation of the inclusion criterion requiring diagnosis within 9 months of screening. This was recorded as a major protocol deviation.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The ocular medical history of the study eye was comparable between treatment arms. All but 2 patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and all patients in the IVT arm reported a history of cataracts (prior cataract surgery or presence of cataract at screening). Overall, 39.3% of patients had a history of dry AMD without geographic atrophy (39.9% in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 38.3% in the IVT arm).

In the targeted non-ocular medical history, the majority of patients had a history of systemic hypertension (66.5% in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 60.5% in the IVT arm). Other prevalent nonocular conditions were diabetes mellitus (19.4% and 14.4% of patients) and coronary artery occlusion/stenosis (11.7% and 9.0%), in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm, respectively.

With regard to prior targeted ocular therapies and treatments, the median number of IVT ranibizumab injections in the study eye in the 9 months prior to first study treatment was 4.0 in the PDS arm and 4.0 in the IVT arm (mean 3.5 versus 3.8 IVT injections). Eight patients in the PDS arm received an additional ranibizumab injection prior to the first study treatment as permitted by the protocol because the implant procedure was delayed, in 2 cases because of scheduling, 2 due to an AE, and 4 because acetyl salicylic acid or NSAIDs had not been interrupted.

Regarding other anti-VEGF treatments administered in the 9 months prior to first study treatment, aflibercept had been administered to 28 patients (11.3%) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 20 (12.0%) in the IVT arm while bevacizumab had been administered to 76 (30.6%) and 51 (30.5%), respectively. An anti-VEGF agent was administered to the fellow eye of 60 patients (24.2%) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 24 (14.4%) in the IVT arm in the 9 months prior to first study treatment. The most frequently used anti-VEGF agent in the fellow eye in both treatment arms was ranibizumab (50 patients [20.2%] in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 19 [11.4%] in the IVT arm).

With regard to prior ocular surgery, cataract surgery in the study eye prior to screening was reported in 143 patients (57.7%) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 104 patients (62.3%) in the IVT arm. The only other prior ocular surgery or procedure reported was yttrium-aluminium garnet (YAG) laser capsulotomy (18 patients [7.3%] in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 25 patients [15.0%] in the IVT arm).

At the end of the study, ocular concomitant medication was reported by 190 patients (190/248 [76.6%]) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and by 63 patients (63/167 [37.7%]) in the IVT arm. The difference between arms was largely driven by short-term use of ocular medications mandated by the protocol before and/or after implant insertion and/or refill-exchange. The use of self-administered antimicrobial ophthalmic drops was required 4 times within 24 hours as per standard of care after PDS implant insertion, and was optional per Investigator's discretion prior to the implant refill-exchange procedure but required as per standard of care after the PDS implant refill-exchange procedure. Antiinflammatory ophthalmic drops were required after implant insertion and implant removal as per standard of care. Topical prednisolone acetate was reported as prednisolone acetate in 27.0% and 4.2% of patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL and IVT arms, respectively, and as prednisolone in 15.3% and 0.6%, respectively; topical oflaxacin in 26.6% and 3.0%, respectively; topical erythromycin in 8.1% and 1.2%, respectively.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary efficacy results

Change from baseline in Best Corrected Visual Acuity (BCVA) score averaged over Week 36 and Week 40 (NI margin: -4.5 letters)

The primary endpoint was the mean change from baseline in BCVA score averaged over Week 36 and Week 40, as assessed using the ETDRS visual acuity chart at a starting distance of 4 meters, with a NI margin of -4.5 letters and equivalence margins of ±4.5 letters, in the Efficacy population.

The primary endpoint was met: the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen was non-inferior (LL CI &gt; -4.5 letters) and equivalent (LL CI &gt; -4.5 letters and UL CI &lt; +4.5 letters) to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen. The difference in adjusted means was -0.3 letters (95.03% CI -1.7, 1.1) - see table below.

Additional supplemental analyses as well as sensitivity analyses on the PP population have been conducted for the primary endpoint.

## Intercurrent Events

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A total of 7 patients (2.8%) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and no patients in the IVT arm experienced at least one intercurrent event through Week 40. The most common intercurrent events were discontinuation of the study due to an AE (5 patients [2.0%] in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm) and receipt of prohibited therapy (4 patients [1.6%] in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm). One patient (0.4%) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm received more than one supplemental treatment in the study eye prior to Week 40.

## Key secondary efficacy endpoints (EMA)

Change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Week 36 and Week 40 (NI margin: -3.9 letters)

Applying a 3.9 letter margin, as requested by the EMA, the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen was noninferior to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, as there was a non-inferior change from baseline in BCVA at the average of Week 36 and Week 40. The difference in adjusted means was -0.3 letters (95.03% CI: -1.7, 1.1), which met the NI criteria (lower limit CI &gt; -3.9 letters).

Change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Week 44 and Week 48 (NI margin: -3.9 letters)

The PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen was non-inferior to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, as measured by change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Week 44 and Week 48. The difference in adjusted means was -0.2 letters (95.03% CI: -1.8, 1.3), which met the NI criteria (LL CI &gt; -3.9 letters).

Change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Week 88 and Week 92 (NI margin: -3.9 letters)

Applying a 3.9 letter margin, as requested by the EMA, the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen was noninferior to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, as there was a non-inferior change from baseline in BCVA at the average of Week 88 and Week 92. The difference in adjusted means was -0.6 letters (95.03% CI: -2.5, 1.3), which met the NI criteria (lower limit CI &gt; -3.9 letters).

## Other secondary efficacy endpoints

Proportion of patients with a BCVA score ≤38 letters/ ≥69 letters at the average of Wk36 and Wk40 and over time

A similar adjusted proportion of patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm had a BCVA score of 38 letters (20/200 approximate Snellen equivalent) or worse averaged over Weeks 36 and 40, 1.2% (3 patients) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm versus 1.8% (3 patients) in the Q4W IVT arm, corresponding to a difference of -0.6% (95.03% CI: -3.1%, 1.8%).

A similar adjusted proportion of patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm had a BCVA score of 69 letters (20/40 approximate Snellen equivalent) or better averaged over Weeks 36 and 40, 80.7% (198 patients) in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm versus 82.1% (134 patients) in the IVT arm, corresponding to a difference of -1.4% (95.03% CI: -7.4%, 4.5%).

The treatment group difference for these endpoints over time through Week 40 was small, and the estimates were consistent with those seen at the average of Weeks 36 and 40 (similar adjusted proportion of patients with a BCVA score of 38 letters or worse and 69 letters or better).

## Change from baseline in CPT (centre point thickness) at Week 36 and over time

CPT change is a surrogate for anatomical response to study treatment and was evaluated at baseline and at Week 36. Mean CPT at baseline was comparable between study arms (176.9 microns in PDS 100 mg/mL arm vs 177.2 microns in IVT arm). The adjusted mean change from baseline at Week 36 was 5.4 microns versus 2.6 microns, corresponding to a difference of 2.8 microns (95.03% CI: -6.2, 11.9).

The change from baseline in CPT was similar between the two arms over time through Week 96. At Week 96, change from baseline was 9.9 microns for the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and -1.3 microns for the IVT arm.

A slight increase in CPT in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm leading up to the scheduled refill-exchange followed by a decrease after the refill-exchange was observed over time. According to the applicant, after each refill-exchange, CPT generally tended to decrease to baseline levels.

## Supplemental treatment with IVT ranibizumab in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm through Week 96

A total of 246 patients were assessed for supplemental treatment at Week 16 and/or Week 20, which excludes 2 patients who discontinued treatment prior to Week 16. During the first treatment interval, before the first scheduled refill-exchange, 4/246 patients (1.6%) received supplemental treatment (1 patient received two supplemental treatments and 3 patients one supplemental treatment), However, 3 of the 4 patients who received supplemental treatment during the first refill-exchange interval received it in error due to a miscalculation in the supplemental treatment criteria (2 patients at Week 16 and 1 patient at both Week 16 and Week 20). 242/246 patients (98.4%) did not receive supplemental treatment.

A total of 241 patients were assessed for supplemental treatment at Week 40 and/or Week 44, which excludes 1 patient who missed both visits and 5 patients who discontinued treatment prior to Week 40 and 1 patient who missed Week 40 visit and discontinued treatment prior to Week 44. During the second treatment interval, before the second scheduled refill-exchange, 13/241 patients (5.4%) received supplemental treatment (4 patients received two supplemental treatments and the rest received one supplemental treatment each), and 228/241 patients (94.6%) did not receive supplemental treatment.

A total of 231 patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm were assessed for supplemental treatment at Week 64 and/or Week 68, which excludes 7 patients who missed both visits, 9 patients who discontinued treatment prior to Week 64, and 1 patient who missed Week 64 visit and discontinued treatment prior to Week 64. During the third treatment interval, 12/231 patients (5.2%) received supplemental treatment (1 patient received 2 supplemental treatments, and the rest received one supplemental treatment each), and 219/231 patients (94.8%) did not receive supplemental treatment.

A total of 225 patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm were assessed for supplemental treatment at Week 88 and/or Week 92, which excludes 6 patients who missed both visits, 16 patients who discontinued treatment prior to Week 88, and 1 patient who missed Week 88 visit and discontinued treatment prior to Week 88. During the fourth treatment interval, 12/225 patients (5.3%) received supplemental treatment (2 patients received 2 supplemental treatments and the rest received one supplemental treatment each), and 213/225 patients (94.7%) did not receive supplemental treatment. One of these patients received supplemental treatment in error at Week 88 after the site proceeded with supplemental treatment based upon their own locally-measured CST.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A total of 246 patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm were assessed for supplemental treatment at least once through Week 96, which excludes 2 patients who discontinued treatment prior to Week 16. Across all four treatment intervals, 31/246 patients (12.6%) received at least one supplemental treatment, and 215/246 patients (87.4%) did not receive supplemental treatment. Of the 31 patients, 18 patients (58.1%) received only one supplement treatment,

Eight patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm received supplemental treatment during multiple treatment intervals.

## Change from Baseline in BCVA averaged over Weeks 60 and 64

No non-inferiority test was pre-specified for the change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Weeks 60 and 64. The PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen was similar to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, as measured by change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Week 60 and Week 64. The difference in adjusted means was 0.4 letters (95.03% CI: 1.4, 2.1).

A sensitivity analysis was performed on the Per Protocol Population, which excluded patients who had major protocol deviations that potentially impacted BCVA scores used in efficacy outcomes. A total of 26 patients were excluded from the Per Protocol Population in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 13 patients in the IVT arm. The results of the sensitivity analysis supported the results of the main analysis, with an adjusted mean difference in the change from baseline in BCVA of 0.3 letters (95.03% CI: -1.5, 2.1) averaged over Week 60 and Week 64.

## Change from baseline in BCVA over time through Week 96

Through Week 96, the change in BCVA from baseline in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm was generally comparable to the IVT arm after Week 8 (post-surgical recovery), when patients who received PDS 100 mg/mL patients returned to their baseline BCVA score.

During the post-operative period, patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm experienced on average a loss of less than 5 letters from baseline BCVA due to the surgical procedure related to PDS. This drop would not be attributed to worsening of nAMD as CPT and CST remained stable through Week 96. After postsurgical recovery, VA was maintained through Week 96.

## Exploratory efficacy endpoints

Change from baseline in CST (central subfield thickness) at Week 36 and over time

The adjusted mean change from baseline in CST was similar between the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm at Week 36 (10.3 microns vs. 4.4 microns, corresponding to a difference in adjusted means of 5.9 microns [95.03% CI: -2.9, 14.7].

The adjusted mean change from baseline in CST was overall comparable between the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm over time through Week 96 (Figure). At Week 96, change from baseline was 18.6 microns for the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and -0.2 microns for the IVT arm.

A slight increase in CST (average change up to approximately 20 microns) leading up to each schedule refill-exchange followed by a decrease after each refill-exchange was observed through Week 96. After each refill-exchange, CST decreased relative to the level at the time of refill-exchange.

Primary endpoint - Change from baseline in BCVA score averaged over Week 36 and Week 40

The differences between the 2 treatment arms in change from baseline in BCVA averaged over Weeks 36 and 40 were similar for age subgroups (&lt;65 years, 65 to &lt;75 years, ≥75 years), sex subgroups (male/female), subgroups of patients with different number of prior anti-VEGF IVT injections (&lt;5 vs. ≥5 prior injections), and subgroups according to baseline BCVA score (&lt;74 vs. ≥74).

The subgroup findings were overall consistent with the primary endpoint analysis.

## Pre-defined and post-hoc sensitivity analyses

Sensitivity analyses for the primary endpoint BCVA averaged over Week 36 and Week 40

The results of the sensitivity analysis in the Per Protocol Population (excluding patients who had major protocol deviations that potentially impacted efficacy outcomes) supported the results of the primary analysis, with an adjusted mean difference in the change from baseline in BCVA of -0.4 letters (95.03% CI: -1.8, 1.1) at the average of Weeks 36 and 40 (see Table 40 above).

To note, one patient was excluded from the PP Population due to receiving supplemental treatment in error prior to Week 40, which was not classified as a major protocol deviation.

Supplementary analyses for the primary endpoint BCVA averaged over Week 36 and Week 40

A trimmed mean analysis was performed by trimming 20% of patients with the worst outcomes from both arms including those who met the 'must-be-trimmed criteria' (refer to SAP for details).

Six patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm met the 'must-be-trimmed' criteria and were considered to have the worst outcomes. After 20% of patients with worst outcomes were trimmed, the adjusted mean change from baseline in BCVA score was similar between the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm (2.8 vs. 3.3 letters, respectively), with a difference in adjusted means of -0.5 letters (95.03% CI: -1.4, 0.4), which was consistent with the results of the primary endpoint analysis.

Two additional supplementary analyses were performed on the primary EP, using the same MMRM model, but different handling rules for two of the intercurrent events.

In Method 1, assessments at any time point either after 2 or more supplemental treatments or after prohibited treatments in the study eye were imputed using the last post-baseline observation prior to such intercurrent event. Using this method, the change in BCVA at the average of Week 36 and Week

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

40 with the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen remained non-inferior and equivalent to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, which was consistent with the results of the primary efficacy endpoint analysis (see above).

In Method 2, assessments either after 2 or more supplemental treatments were administered or after prohibited treatments were taken were excluded. Using this method, the change in BCVA at the average of Week 36 and Week 40 with the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen remained non-inferior and equivalent to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, which was consistent with the results of the primary efficacy endpoint analysis (see above).

Sensitivity and supplementary analyses for the key secondary endpoint BCVA averaged over Week 44 and Week 48

For this key secondary EP, additional supplemental analyses as well as sensitivity analyses on the PP population have been conducted, all supporting the results from the main analysis in the Efficacy Population (see table below).

## Ancillary analyses

Proportion of patients with a gain/ loss of 15 letters in visual function over time

At the average of Weeks 36 and 40, a similar adjusted proportion of patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm had a loss of &lt;15 letters (97.6% [240 patients] vs. 96.9% [158 patients], respectively), and a gain of &gt;15 letters (1.6% [4 patients] vs. 1.2% [2 patients], respectively) from baseline in a post hoc analysis.

Post-hoc analyses showed that at the average of Weeks 44 and 48, a similar adjusted proportion of patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm had a loss of &lt;15 letters (97.5% [238 patients] vs. 96.3% [155 patients], respectively), and a gain of &gt;15 letters (1.6% [4 patients] vs. 1.9% [3 patients], respectively) from baseline.

At the average of Weeks 88 and 92, a similar proportion of patients in the PDS 100 mg/mL arm and the IVT arm also had a loss of &lt;15 letters (95.3% vs. 93.6%, corresponding to a difference of 1.7% (CI 95.03%: -3.0, 6.4) or a gain of &gt; 15 letters (2.6% vs. 3.8%, corresponding to a difference of - 1.3% [CI 95.03%: -4.8, 2.3]) from baseline.

The treatment group differences over time through Week 96 were generally small and were consistent with the findings at the average of Week 36 and 40.

Post-hoc supplementary analysis for primary endpoint (upon EMA request)

As requested by EMA during the previous MAA, a hypothetical estimand was utilized for all four intercurrent events and a missing not at random (MNAR) approach was used for missing data. The data following the intercurrent events were censored and a Bayesian Bootstrap Approximation method was used to multiply impute the endpoint (n = 100 imputations) from among the 10% of worse performing patients without an intercurrent event in the same arm. The analysis was conducted using an ANCOVA model and averaged subject assessments over Weeks 36 and 40.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Using this method, the change in BCVA at the average of Weeks 36 and 40 with the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen remained non-inferior to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, with a difference of -0.2 (1.4, 1.1). Similarly, the difference stays within the NI margins using the averages of weeks 44 and 48 (-0.3, CI: -1.7 to 1) and of weeks 88 and 92 (-0.9, CI: -2.8, 0.9).

Post-hoc supplementary analysis for the key secondary endpoints (upon EMA request)

The same supplementary analysis was also conducted for the 2 key secondary endpoints Weeks 44 and 48 and Weeks 88 and 92.

Using this method, the change in BCVA at the average of Week 44 and Week 48 and at the average of Week 88 and Week 92 with the PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W regimen remained non-inferior to the IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg Q4W regimen, which was consistent with the results of the main analysis.

## 5.3.3. Clinical studies in special populations

N/A

## 5.3.4. Supportive study

Study GR40549 (Portal) is a multicentre, open-label, VA-masked, multiple-cohort extension study designed with the primary objective of evaluating the long-term safety and tolerability of ranibizumab 100 mg/mL delivered via the PDS administered Q24W to patients who were eligible to enrol in the extension study from Study GX28228 or Study GR40548 (also referred to as 'parent studies'). In addition, patients from the ongoing Phase IIIb study WR42221 (Velodrome) have the possibility to join study GR40549. However, data from these patients are not included in the submitted analyses.

With this submission, long-term data with a CCOD of 31 January 2025 are reported for patients with PDS who enrolled into the long-term extension Study GR40549. The GR40549 Population presented in the Summary of Clinical Efficacy for Susvimo includes all patients who received at least one study treatment in Study GR40549 (ranibizumab 100 mg/mL Q24W), except patients who were at the noncompliant site 290667 in GX28228, patients from the GX28228 OAT sub-study, or patients from Study WR42221.

Secondary endpoints in Study GR40549 provide additional complementary long-term efficacy information to the data generated from Studies GR40548 and GX28228. Since the trial is an extension study with no comparator arm, no hypothesis testing is conducted.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Key Inclusion and Exclusion Criteria

Patients were required to meet the following criteria for study entry:

- Previous enrolment in and completion of Study GX28228 or Study GR40548 without early treatment or study discontinuation in either study (monthly IVT ranibizumab 0.5 mg or implant arms)

Patients who met any of the following criteria were excluded from study entry:

- Continuous use of any prohibited medications or treatments

## Efficacy endpoints

For Study GR40549, efficacy data are summarized descriptively for patients who completed parent Studies GX28228 or GR40548 and were then treated in Study GR40549. All patients received PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W after enrolment into Study GR40549, regardless of their parent study treatment allocation (including the patients in the IVT arms who were implanted in Study GR40549).

Long-term efficacy is descriptively summarized mainly through the change in BCVA and CPT from baseline as well as the number of supplemental treatments received (all secondary endpoints) and change in CST from baseline (exploratory endpoint). These data are summarized at each GR40549 timepoint until the CCOD (31 January 2025) by arm, using descriptive statistics, including 95% confidence intervals. The last observation carried forward is used for intermittent missing values during Study GR40549 to correct for the difference in visit schedules between arms. Due to the nature of this single-arm extension study and the primary objective to evaluate long-term safety, no formal hypothesis testing is conducted.

Summaries are based on all observed data in the GR40549 population. This corresponds to the treatment policy estimand.

## Analysis Populations

The GR40549 Population (used for all efficacy summaries) through the CCOD of 31 January 2025 comprised a total of 541 patients who enrolled and were treated in Study GR40549 following completion of a parent study. Within the GR40549 Population, 189 patients enrolled from Study GX28228 (excluding patients in the OAT sub-study), and 352 patients enrolled from Study GR40548 are included for evaluation in this submission.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

For Study GR40549, efficacy data are summarized for patients who completed parent Studies GX28228 or GR40548 and were treated in Study GR40549. Data are summarized for patients from the GR40548 parent study based on GR40548 baseline, as well as the GR40549 baseline. Data for patients from the GX28228 parent study are summarized based on the GR40549 baseline only.

All patients received the study treatment of PDS 100 mg/mL Q24W after enrolment into study GR40549, regardless of their parent study treatment allocation (including the IVT patients who were implanted in Study GR40549). The treatment arms are reported as per their parent study arms:

- GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL arm (N = 220)
- GR40548 IVT arm (N = 132)
- GX28228 PDS 10 mg/mL arm (N = 49)
- GX28228 PDS 40 mg/mL arm (N = 55)
- GX28228 PDS 100 mg/mL arm (N = 56)
- GX28228 IVT arm (N = 29)

## Patient Disposition

In the GR40549 Population, only patients that enrolled into and were treated in GR40549 were included. Therefore, patient disposition is reported through the GR40549 study only.

All patients from GX28228 have either completed (146/189 [77.2%]) or discontinued (43/189 [22.8%]) Study GR40549. The last patient from GX28228 completed Study GR40549 in November 2023.

The most common reasons for early discontinuation from Study GR40549 for GX28228 patients were withdrawal by subject (17/189 [9.0%]), and death (16/189 [8.5%]). The other reasons for study discontinuation were due to physician decision (3/189 [1.6%]), other (3/189 [1.6%]; 2 patients were discontinued due to IRB expiration of the trial at a site, and 1 patient due to early termination (per the primary investigator, the subject could not wait for supplemental treatment evaluation visit, disease activity and visual acuity decreased), lost to follow-up (2/189 [1.1%]), or adverse event (1/189 [0.5%]). No patient discontinued study due to lack of efficacy.

As of the CCOD of 31 January 2025, 273 out of the 352 patients (77.6%) from Study GR40548 were still enrolled in Study GR40549. Seventy-nine out of the 352 patients (22.4%) discontinued study GR40549 early. The most common reasons for early discontinuation from Study GR40549 for GR40548 patients were withdrawal by subject (29/352 [8.2%]) and death (24/352 [6.8%]). The other reasons for study discontinuations were other (13/352 [3.7%]), lost to follow-up (5/352 [1.4%]), adverse event (4/352 [1.1%]), and physician decision (4/352 [1.1%]). No patient cited lack of efficacy as the reason for study discontinuation.

Overall, 122/541 (22.6%) patients who enrolled from either the GR40548 or the GX28228 parent study, discontinued Study GR40549 early. The majority of these discontinuations were due to withdrawal by subject (46/541 [8.5%]) and death (40/541 [7.4%]).

Patients who discontinued study treatment were encouraged to remain in the study and attend as many scheduled study visits as possible. Overall, 173 out of the 541 patients (32.0%) from both parent studies discontinued study treatment early in GR40549. The majority of these discontinuations were due to adverse event (58/541 [10.7%]), withdrawal by subject (42/541 [7.8%]), and death (39/541 [7.2%]).

## PDS Exposure

## Patients from Study GR40548 using GR40548 Baseline

The mean duration of study treatment was 270 weeks and the mean (SD) number of study treatments per patient was 11.3 (2.7) in the Study GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL arm.

The GR40548 IVT arm patients were not implanted with PDS until they enrolled into GR40549; this arm is summarized using the GR40549 baseline.

## Patients from Study GR40548 using GR40549 Baseline

The mean duration of study treatment was 174 weeks in the Study GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 190 weeks in the GR40548 IVT arm.

The mean (SD) number of study treatments per patient was 7.3 (2.7) in the GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL arm and 8.1 (2.1) in the GR40548 IVT arm.

## Patients from Study GX28228 using GR40549 Baseline

The mean duration of study treatment ranged from 201-210 weeks in the three PDS arms from Study GX28228 and was 241 weeks for patients from the GX28228 IVT arm (see Table below for a detailed breakdown).

The mean number of treatments per patient ranged from 8.2-8.9 in the three PDS arms from GX28228 and was 9.8 for patients from the GX28228 IVT arm.

## Long-Term Efficacy Data

Study GR40549 is an open-label extension study; the primary objectives and corresponding endpoints are related to the safety of PDS. All endpoints corresponding to efficacy objectives are categorized as secondary or exploratory. Because GR40549 is an extension study with no comparator arm, no formal hypothesis testing is conducted for the efficacy outcomes. Efficacy summaries are based on all observed data in the GR40549 Population. This corresponds to the treatment policy estimand.

Efficacy analyses for Study GR40549 are presented separately for patients from parent studies GR40548 and GX28228. For patients from the GR40548 parent study, efficacy data are analysed and presented from both the GR40548 baseline (before the administration of the first study treatment in Study GR40548), and the GR40549 baseline (after completion of parent Study GR40548, and before the administration of the first study treatment in study GR40549). For patients from Study GX28228, efficacy data are analysed and presented from the GR40549 baseline (after completion of Study GX28228 and before the administration of the first study treatment in Study GR40549).

For patients from Study GR40548, the duration of follow-up from GR40548 baseline is up to 324 weeks. However, summaries and plots are discussed up to Week 288 from GR40548 baseline (corresponding to Week 192 from GR40549 baseline) due to the ongoing nature of data collection and the corresponding low sample size after this timepoint.

For patients from Study GX28228, the length of follow-up in GR40549 after completion of GX28228 is 240 weeks (end of GR40549 follow-up per protocol).

Change in BCVA Score from Baseline Over Time

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Patients from Study GR40548 using GR40548 Baseline

Both arms showed a similar small gradual decrease in BCVA through the additional ~4 years for patients from Study GR40548 in Study GR40549, with mean BCVA score changes (95% CI) of -9.5 letters (-12.0, -7.0) and -7.4 letters (-10.4, -4.4) at Week 288 in the GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL and IVT arms, respectively (Figure 10). The median BCVA score change at Week 288 was -6.0 letters and - 3.5 letters for patients from the GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL and IVT arms, respectively, which is approximately 1 line of vision loss. BCVA score was assessed using the ETDRS Visual Acuity Chart at a starting distance of 4 meters.

The mean (SD) BCVA score at Week 288 was 65.2 (17.0) letters and 69.2 (16.5) letters (20/50 and 20/40 approximate Snellen Equivalent) for patients from the GR40548 PDS 100 mg/mL and IVT arms, respectively.

## Patients from Study GX28228 using GR40549 Baseline

Mean BCVA score change from baseline (95% CI) at Week 240 in Study GR40549 for patients from Study GX28228 were: -1.5 letters (-7.1, 4.1) for the GX28228 PDS 10 mg/mL arm, -16.3 letters (- 22.6, -10.0) for the GX28228 PDS 40 mg/mL arm, -12.8 letters (-18.8, -6.9) for the GX28228 PDS 100 mg/mL arm, and -9.1 letters (-13.6, -4.5) for the IVT GX28228 arm. The median BCVA score change from baseline was 0.5, -11.0, -8.0, and -7.0 letters at Week 240, in the 10 mg/mL, 40 mg/mL, 100 mg/mL and IVT arms, respectively. It is important to note that the baseline BCVA scores at entry into Study GR40549 differed across arms (ranging from 64.9 letters in the GX28228 PDS 10 mg/mL arm to 75.2 letters in the GX28228 IVT arm) and the sample size in these arms was small, limiting any meaningful data interpretation. The smaller mean BCVA score change from baseline observed for the GX28228 PDS 10 mg/mL arm may be partially explained by these factors.
